##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ‘जज्ञानः' इत्येकादशर्चमष्टमं सूक्तं काण्वस्य कुरुसुतेरार्षम् । आद्या नव गायत्र्यो दशमी बृहत्येकादशी सतोबृहती । इंद्रो देवता । तथा चानुक्रांतं- जज्ञान एकादश प्रगाथांतम्' इति । महाव्रते निष्केवल्ये 'जज्ञानो नु शतक्रतुः' इत्येषा । तथैव पंचमारण्यके सूत्रितं- जज्ञानो नु शतक्रतुरित्येका' (ऐ. आ. ५. २. ३) इति

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning जज्ञानः (*jajñānaḥ*) has eleven verses; it is the eighth sūkta and its seer is Kurusuti of the Kaṇva line. The first nine verses are in Gāyatrī, the tenth in Bṛhatī, the eleventh in Satobṛhatī. The deity is Indra. So the Anukramaṇī says: जज्ञान एकादश प्रगाथांतम् (*jajñāna ekādaśa pragāthāṃtam*). In the Mahāvrata, in the Niṣkevalya, the verse जज्ञानो नु शतक्रतुः (*jajñāno nu śatakratuḥ*) is recited. So also it is stated in the fifth Āraṇyaka: जज्ञानो नु शतक्रतुरित्येका (*jajñāno nu śatakraturityekā*) (Ai. Ā. 5. 2. 3).**NOTE**

This sūkta beginning जज्ञानः (*jajñānaḥ*) is the eighth sūkta in the eighth Anuvāka. It has eleven Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Kurusuti, born in the Kaṇva line. The first nine Ṛks of this sūkta are in the Gāyatrī metre, the tenth in Bṛhatī, the eleventh in Satobṛhatī. Indra is the deity; so it is said in the Anukramaṇī: जज्ञान एकादश प्रगथांतं (*jajñāna ekādaśa pragathāṃtaṃ*). At the Mahāvrata sacrifice, when reciting the Niṣkevalya śastra mantras, this Ṛk beginning जज्ञानो नु शतक्रतुः (*jajñāno nu śatakratuḥ*) is to be recited, as is explained in the fifth Āraṇyaka of the Aitareya Āraṇyaka by the sentence जज्ञानो नु शतक्रतुरित्येषा (*jajñāno nu śatakraturityeṣā*) (Ai. Ā. 5-2-7).

Sūkta—77
Maṇḍala—8 ‖ Anuvāka—8 ‖ Sūkta—77 ‖
Aṣṭaka—6 ‖ Adhyāya—5 ‖ Varga—29, 30 ‖
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta—11 ‖
Ṛṣi—Kurusutiḥ Kāṇvaḥ ‖
Devatā—Indraḥ ‖
Chandas—1-9, Gāyatrī; 10, Bṛhatī; 11, Satobṛhatī ‖

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ज॒ज्ञा॒नो नु श॒तक्र॑तु॒र्वि पृ॑च्छ॒दिति॑ मा॒तर॑म् ।  
> क उ॒ग्राः के ह॑ शृण्विरे ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 159

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> जज्ञानः । नु । शतऽक्रतुः । वि । पृच्छत् । इति । मातरम् ।  
> के । उग्राः । के । ह । शृण्विरे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयमिंद्रः जज्ञानो नु जायमान एव शतक्रतुः बहुकर्मा इति इत्थं मातरं स्वजननीं विपृच्छति । किमिति । क उग्राः उद्गूर्णबला लोके । के ह श्रृण्विरे श्रूयंते गुणैः । के विश्रुता इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra, Śatakratu, of many works, as soon as he is born, asks his mother in this way: who are the fierce ones, of uplifted strength, in the world, and who are heard of for their qualities, that is, who are renowned?

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उग्राः (*ugrāḥ*)—those possessed of exceeding strength
- के के ह (*ke ke ha*)—who, who
- शृण्विरे (*śṛṇvire*)—are renowned
- इति (*iti*)—thus
- जज्ञानो नु (*jajñāno nu*)—immediately on being born
- शतक्रतुः (*śatakratuḥ*)—Indra, doer of many works
- मातरं (*mātaraṃ*)—his mother
- वि पृच्छत् (*vi pṛcchat*)—asked

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, doer of many works, immediately on being born asked his mother who are the mighty and powerful ones of exceeding strength, and who are renowned.

##### English translation
As soon as he was born Satakratu asked his mother-Who are the mighty, who are renowned ?

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आदीं॑ शव॒स्य॑ब्रवीदौर्णवा॒भम॑ही॒शुव॑म् ।  
> ते पु॑त्र संतु नि॒ष्टुरः॑ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आत् । ईम् । शवसी । अब्रवीत् । और्णऽवाभम् । अहीशुवम् ।  
> ते । पुत्र । संतु । निःऽतुरः ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 160

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रेण पृष्टा शवसी माता अनंतरमेव एनमिंद्रम् अब्रवीत् । किमिति उच्यते । और्णवाभमहीशुवम् एतन्नामानावसुरौ तिष्ठतः । तावुक्तावन्ये च तादृशा हे पुत्र तव निष्टुरः निस्तारणीयाः संतु इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Questioned by Indra, his mother Śavasī at once said to him: there are two asuras named और्णवाभमहीशुवम् (*aurṇavābhamahīśuvam*), and others like them; O son, let them be ones to be overcome by you.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुत्र (*putra*)—O child
- और्णवाभं (*aurṇavābhaṃ*)—the one called Aurṇavābha
- अहीशुवं (*ahīśuvaṃ*)—and the one called Ahīśuva, the two
- ते (*te*)—by you
- निष्टुरः (*niṣṭuraḥ*)—to be harshly conquered
- संतु (*saṃtu*)—are, thus
- आत् ईं (*āt īṃ*)—as soon as asked by Indra
- शवसी (*śavasī*)—the mother of Indra, full of strength
- अब्रवीत् (*abravīt*)—said to her son

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As soon as she was asked by Indra, the mother of Indra, full of strength, said to her son: O child, the one called Aurṇavābha and the one called Ahīśuva, these two are to be conquered harshly by you.

##### English translation
His strong mother answered,—Aurnavabha and Ahishuva, be these my son, the foes whom you shall overcome.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> समित्तान्वृ॑त्र॒हाखि॑द॒त्खे अ॒राँ इ॑व॒ खेद॑या ।  
> प्रवृ॑द्धो दस्यु॒हाभ॑वत् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सम् । इत् । तान् । वृत्रऽहा । अखिदत् । खे । अरान्ऽइव । खेदया ।  
> प्रऽवृद्धः । दस्युऽहा । अभवत् ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 161

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तान् जनन्योक्तान् वृत्रहा इंद्रः समित् सहैव अखिदत्। खेदनं नाम आकर्षणम् । खे रथचक्रस्य नाभौ अरान् चक्रांगभूतांछंकून् खेदया रज्ज्वा इव । तया तान् यथा संखिदंति तद्वत् । तथा कृत्वा दस्युहा शत्रुघातींद्रः प्रवृद्धः अभवत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, the slayer of Vṛtra, dragged together those spoken of by his mother. Khedana means dragging. As the spokes, the parts of a wheel, in the nave (kha) of a chariot wheel are drawn together by a rope (khedayā), so he drew them together; having done so, Indra, the slayer of Dasyus and destroyer of enemies, became greatly grown.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृत्रहा (*vṛtrahā*)—Indra, the slayer of Vṛtra
- समित् (*samit*)—as soon as thus spoken to by his mother
- खे (*khe*)—on the rim of the chariot wheel
- अरान् (*arān*)—the spokes
- खेदया इव (*khedayā iva*)—as one ties with a rope and drags
- तान् (*tān*)—those enemies
- अखिदत् (*akhidat*)—dragged along with a scraping noise
- दस्युहा (*dasyuhā*)—the same Indra, destroyer of enemies
- प्रवृद्धः (*pravṛddhaḥ*)—endowed with growth
- अभवत् (*abhavat*)—became

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, the slayer of Vṛtra, as soon as he was thus spoken to by his mother, dragged those enemies along with a scraping noise, as one ties the spokes with a rope on the rim of a chariot wheel and drags them. The same Indra, destroyer of enemies, then became endowed with growth.

##### English translation
The slayer of Vritra dragged them along as spokes (are tied fast) with a rope in the nave of a chariot wheel: he swelled in vigour, the slayer of enemies.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> एक॑या प्रति॒धापि॑बत्सा॒कं सरां॑सि त्रिं॒शत॑म् ।  
> इंद्रः॒ सोम॑स्य काणु॒का ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एकया । प्रतिऽधा । अपिबत् । साकम् । सरांसि । त्रिंशतम् ।  
> इंद्रः । सोमस्य । काणुका ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

### Printed p. 162

> अयम् इंद्रः एकया एकेन प्रतिधा प्रतिधानेन साकम् एकधैव त्रिंशतम् अपि । उक्थपात्राणीत्यर्थः । कीदृशानि । सरांसि सोमस्य पूर्णानि सोमरसेन पूर्णानि काणुका कांतानि क्रांतानि वा सोमेन कृतानि वा सोमपूर्णानि अपिबत् पीतवान् माध्यंदिनसवने । याज्ञिकप्रसिद्ध्यैवम् । नैरुक्तप्रसिद्ध्या तु कालाभिमानींद्रः । त्रिंशदपरपक्षस्याहोरात्रास्त्रिंशत् पूर्वपक्षस्य च संति । तानेकरूपमनुभवतीति । एतत्सर्वम् ‘एकेन प्रतिधानेनापिबत्' (निरु. ५. ११) इत्यादिनिरुक्ते तद्व्याख्याने च स्पष्टमुक्तम् । तदत्र द्रष्टव्यम् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra drank thirty vessels at once, with one pratidhā, that is, with one drawing; the meaning is, the uktha vessels. Of what kind? Lakes (sarāṃsi) filled with Soma, that is, filled with Soma juice, kāṇukā, that is, delightful or traversed, or made by Soma, or full of Soma. He drank them at the midday pressing. So it is according to the sacrificial tradition. According to the Nairukta tradition, however, Indra is the presiding power of time: of the dark half there are thirty days and nights, and of the bright half also; he experiences them as one form. All this is clearly stated in the Nirukta passage एकेन प्रतिधानेनापिबत् (*ekena pratidhānenāpibat*) and the rest (Ni. 5. 11), and in its commentary; it is to be seen there.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सरांसि (*sarāṃsi*)—those that are like lakes
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—filled with Soma
- काणुका (*kāṇukā*)—attractive
- त्रिंशतं (*triṃśataṃ*)—the Soma of thirty vessels
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- एकया प्रतिधा (*ekayā pratidhā*)—with a single draught
- साकं (*sākaṃ*)—in one go
- अपिबत् (*apibat*)—drank

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra drank in one go, with a single draught, the Soma of thirty vessels, which are like lakes, full of Soma and attractive.

##### English translation
At one draught Indra drank at once thrity lakes filled with Soma.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Kāṇukā: to state the meaning of this word, Yāska has given an explanation of this Ṛk.

एकेन प्रतिधानेनापिबत् साकं सहेत्यर्थः । इंद्रः सोमस्य काणुका कांतकानीति वा क्रांतकानीति वा कृतकानीति वा . (*ekena pratidhānenāpibat sākaṃ sahetyarthaḥ | iṃdraḥ somasya kāṇukā kāṃtakānīti vā krāṃtakānīti vā kṛtakānīti vā .*) **GLOSS**

इंद्रः सोमस्य कांत इति वा कणेः घात इति वा कणेः हतः कांतियुतः ॥ (*iṃdraḥ somasya kāṃta iti vā kaṇeḥ ghāta iti vā kaṇeḥ hataḥ kāṃtiyutaḥ ‖*) **GLOSS**

तत्रैतदाज्ञिका वेदयंते । त्रिंशदुष्ठपात्राणि माध्यंदिने सवन एकदेवतानि तान्येतस्मिन् काल एकेन प्रतिधानेन पिबति तान्यत्र सरांग्युच्यंते ॥ (*tatraitadājñikā vedayaṃte | triṃśaduṣṭhapātrāṇi mādhyaṃdine savana ekadevatāni tānyetasmin kāla ekena pratidhānena pibati tānyatra sarāṃgyucyaṃte ‖*) **GLOSS**

त्रिंशदपरपक्षस्याहोरात्रास्त्रिंशत् पूर्वपक्षस्येति नैरुक्ताः । तस्या एता चांद्रमस्य आगामिस्य आप्यो भवंति रश्मयस्ता अपरपक्षे पिबति । तथापि (*triṃśadaparapakṣasyāhorātrāstriṃśat pūrvapakṣasyeti nairuktāḥ | tasyā etā cāṃdramasya āgāmisya āpyo bhavaṃti raśmayastā aparapakṣe pibati | tathāpi*) **GLOSS**
**NOTE**

### Printed p. 163

निगमो भवति—यमुक्षितिमुक्षितयः पिबंति इति । तं पूर्वपक्ष आप्यायंति तथापि निगमो भवति—यथा देवा अंशुमाप्याययंति इति ॥ (*nigamo bhavati—yamukṣitimukṣitayaḥ pibaṃti iti | taṃ pūrvapakṣa āpyāyaṃti tathāpi nigamo bhavati—yathā devā aṃśumāpyāyayaṃti iti ‖*) (Ni. 5-11) **GLOSS**

The word काणुका (*kāṇukā*) has several meanings. Thus: कांतकानीति वा प्रियाणीत्यर्थः (*kāṃtakānīti vā priyāṇītyarthaḥ*)—desired, or pleasing; or क्रांतकानीति वा सोमस्य पूर्णानीत्यर्थः (*krāṃtakānīti vā somasya pūrṇānītyarthaḥ*)—filled, that is, full of Soma juice; or कृतकानीति वा (*kṛtakānīti vā*), संस्कृतानि ऋत्विग्भिः इंद्रार्थमेव (*saṃskṛtāni ṛtvigbhiḥ iṃdrārthameva*)—consecrated by the priests with mantras for the drinking of Indra. Thus three kinds of meaning have been stated. These are meanings made on the view that all of them are qualifiers of the word सरांसि (*sarāṃsi*). The meaning is that Indra drank in one gulp Soma juice of the qualities stated above, filling thirty lakes. साकं (*sākaṃ*) means सह (*saha*), at one time. He did not drink sipping little by little; the idea is that he drank all of it at one time, in one gulp.

Or, if the word काणुकाः (*kāṇukāḥ*) is taken as a qualifier of Indra—इंद्रः सोमस्य कांत इति वा (*iṃdraḥ somasya kāṃta iti vā*): Indra is fond of the Soma juice, he loves the Soma juice; or कणे, घात इति वा कणे हतः कांतिहत ।ः कांतिरभिलाष इत्यर्थः। (*kaṇe, ghāta iti vā kaṇe hataḥ kāṃtihata |ḥ kāṃtirabhilāṣa ityarthaḥ|*)**NOTE** Indra drinks as he pleases until his desire is fulfilled. Thus two kinds of meaning can be stated.

सरांसि त्रिंशतं (*sarāṃsi triṃśataṃ*)—here the word सरांसि (*sarāṃsi*) means vessels. Yāska has said that two kinds of meaning can be given to these words. The sacrificial view, that is, those expert in sacrifices and the like, speak thus—

त्रिंशदुक्थपात्राणि माध्यंदिने सवन एकदेवतानि । तत्र हि माध्यंदिने सवने उक्थपर्यायाः त्रयस्त्रोऽस्यैंद्रा एव भवंति त्रिष्वपि चैतेषु दशदश चनुसः । तदभिप्रायेण त्रिंशत्सरांसीत्युक्तं मंत्रदृशा ॥ (*triṃśadukthapātrāṇi mādhyaṃdine savana ekadevatāni | tatra hi mādhyaṃdine savane ukthaparyāyāḥ trayastro 'syaiṃdrā eva bhavaṃti triṣvapi caiteṣu daśadaśa canusaḥ | tadabhiprāyeṇa triṃśatsarāṃsītyuktaṃ maṃtradṛśā ‖*) (Niru. Vyākhyā)**NOTE**

At the midday pressing, the thirty vessels called uktha-vessels are all prepared with one deity (Indra) in view. There is a custom of calling those vessels सरांसि (*sarāṃsi*). Therefore it is said here that Indra drank in one gulp the Soma filling the thirty vessels prepared for Indra at the midday pressing. This is the view of the sacrificial authorities.

Now the view of the second party is as follows: in a month there are two fortnights, the earlier fortnight and the later fortnight. One fortnight is fifteen days and fifteen nights, in all

### Printed p. 164

thirty. In the thirty days and nights of the later fortnight the rays drink the Soma that is in the moon. In this matter—

यथा देवा अंशुमाप्याययंति यमक्षितिमक्षितयः पिबंति । तेन चेंद्रो वरुणो बृहस्पतिराप्याययंतु भुवनस्य गोपाः ॥ (*yathā devā aṃśumāpyāyayaṃti yamakṣitimakṣitayaḥ pibaṃti | tena ceṃdro varuṇo bṛhaspatirāpyāyayaṃtu bhuvanasya gopāḥ ‖*) **GLOSS**

there is also the support of this Vedic text. This is the view of the authors of the Nirukta.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒भि गं॑ध॒र्वम॑तृणदबु॒ध्नेषु॒ रज॒स्स्वा ।  
> इंद्रो॑ ब्र॒ह्मभ्य॒ इद्वृ॒धे ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अभि । गंधर्वम् । अतृणत् । अबुध्नेषु । रजःऽसु । आ ।  
> इंद्रः । ब्रह्मऽभ्यः । इत् । वृधे ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयम् इंद्रः गंधर्वम् । गामुदकं धारयतीति गंधर्वो मेघः । तम् अभि आ अतृणत् सर्वतो हिंसितवान् । कुत्र । अबुध्नेषु पदनिधानयोग्यस्थानरहितेषु रजःसु लोकेषु । अंतरिक्षप्रदेशेष्वित्यर्थः । किमर्थम् । ब्रह्मभ्य इत् ब्राह्मणेभ्य एव वृधे वर्धनाय ॥ ॥ २९ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra struck down all around the Gandharva; the Gandharva is the cloud, which holds (dhārayati) the water (go). Where? In the realms (rajas), the worlds, called abudhna, devoid of any place fit for setting the foot; that is, in the regions of the atmosphere. For what purpose? For the increase of the Brāhmaṇas alone.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अबुध्नेषु (*abudhneṣu*)—where there is no support at all for setting the foot
- रजःसु (*rajaḥsu*)—in the realms of the atmosphere
- ब्रह्मभ्य इत् (*brahmabhya it*)—of the knowers of Brahman alone
- वृधे (*vṛdhe*)—for the increase (for the sake of rain)
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- गंधर्वं (*gaṃdharvaṃ*)—the cloud
- अभ्यातृणत् (*abhyātṛṇat*)—split asunder

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In the realms of the atmosphere, where there is no support at all for setting the foot, Indra split the cloud asunder to pour down rain, for the increase of the knowers of Brahman alone.

### Printed p. 165

##### English translation
In the realms (of the sky) where the foot finds no resting-place, Indra shattered the cloud to bring increase to the Brahmans.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> निरा॑विध्यद्गि॒रिभ्य॒ आ धा॒रय॑त्प॒क्वमो॑द॒नम् ।  
> इंद्रो॑ बुं॒दं स्वा॑ततम् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> निः । अविध्यत् । गिरिऽभ्यः । आ । धारयत् । पक्वम् । ओदनम् ।  
> इंद्रः । बुंदम् । सुऽआततम् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयम् इंद्रः गिरिभ्यः मेघेभ्यः सकाशादुदकं निर्गमयितुं निराविध्यत् संप्राहरत् तानेव मेघान् । किं कुर्वन् । पक्वं परिपक्वम् ओदनं कुर्वन् मनुष्याणामर्थाय । केन साधनेनेति तदुच्यते । बुंदम् इषुं स्वाततं सुष्ठु सर्वतो विस्तृतमादायेति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra struck down those same clouds (giri), in order to bring the water out of the clouds. Doing what? Making cooked rice (odana) fully ripe for the sake of men. By what means? The remainder is: having taken the arrow (bunda), well spread out on all sides.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- स्वाततं (*svātataṃ*)—very far-stretched
- बुंदं (*buṃdaṃ*)—having discharged the arrow
- गिरिभ्यः (*giribhyaḥ*)—from the clouds
- निराविध्यत् (*nirāvidhyat*)—(splitting them) poured down rain
- पक्वं (*pakvaṃ*)—fully ripe
- ओदनं (*odanaṃ*)—rice
- आ धारयत् (*ā dhārayat*)—secured for men

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, having discharged his very far-stretched arrow, split the clouds and poured down rain. He secured fully ripe rice for men. (He provided it.)

##### English translation
Indra smote (rain) from the clouds with his far-stretched arrow, he secured boiled rice (for men).

### Printed p. 166

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

बुंदं (*buṃdaṃ*)—इषुं (*iṣuṃ*): the arrow. For the word बुंद (*buṃda*) Yāska has given this explanation—

बुंदः । बुंद इषुर्भवति । बुंदो वा भिंदो वा भयदो वा भासमानो द्रवतीति वा । निरविध्यद्गिरिभ्य आधारयत्पक्वमोदनं । इंद्रो बुंदं स्वाततं । निरविध्यद्गिरिभ्य आधारयत्पक्वमोदनमुदकदासं मेघं । इ (*buṃdaḥ | buṃda iṣurbhavati | buṃdo vā bhiṃdo vā bhayado vā bhāsamāno dravatīti vā | niravidhyadgiribhya ādhārayatpakvamodanaṃ | iṃdro buṃdaṃ svātataṃ | niravidhyadgiribhya ādhārayatpakvamodanamudakadāsaṃ meghaṃ | i*) (Ni. 6-34)

Bunda means an arrow. An arrow is called bunda because it pierces the target, or because it causes fear to the enemy, or because it shines when it leaves the bow. The use of this word is in the present Ṛk. The idea of the explanation is that Indra, standing on the mountains, fixed an arrow to the bow there, drew it to the ear, and, able to pour down rain, split the cloud full of abundant water and poured down rain.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> श॒तब्र॑ध्न॒ इषु॒स्तव॑ स॒हस्र॑पर्ण॒ एक॒ इत् ।  
> यमिं॑द्र चकृ॒षे युज॑म् ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शतऽब्रध्नः । इषुः । तव । सहस्रऽपर्णः । एकः । इत् ।  
> यम् । इंद्र । चकृषे । युजम् ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र तव इषुः शतब्रध्नः शताग्रः सहस्रपर्णः सहस्रसंख्याकैः पत्रैः संवृतः शीघ्रगमनाय । अपरिमितगमनो वा । स च एक इत् एक एव । यं च इषुं युजं सहायं चकृषे करोषि युद्धाय ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, your arrow is śatabradhna, with a hundred points, and sahasraparṇa, covered with a thousand feathers for swift going, or of unlimited going. And it is one alone. It is the arrow which you make your companion (yuj), your ally, for battle.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- यं (*yaṃ*)—which arrow
- युजं (*yujaṃ*)—as the means for your battle
- चकृषे (*cakṛṣe*)—you make
- एक इत् (*eka it*)—that single one
- तव इषुः (*tava iṣuḥ*)—your arrow

### Printed p. 167

- शतब्रध्नः (*śatabradhnaḥ*)—with a hundred sharp points
- सहस्रपर्णः (*sahasraparṇaḥ*)—with a thousand leaves (feathers)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, that single arrow of yours, which you make the means for your battle, has a hundred sharp points and a thousand feathers.

##### English translation
That single shaft of yours, Indra, which you make your ally, is hundred-pointed, thousand-feathered.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तेन॑ स्तो॒तृभ्य॒ आ भ॑र॒ नृभ्यो॒ नारि॑भ्यो॒ अत्त॑वे ।  
> स॒द्यो जा॒त ऋ॑भुष्ठिर ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तेन । स्तोतृऽभ्यः । आ । भर । नृऽभ्यः । नारिऽभ्यः । अत्तवे ।  
> सद्यः । जातः । ऋभुऽस्थिर ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तेन इषुणा स्तोतृभ्यः अस्मभ्यं नृभ्यः मनुष्येभ्यः । पुत्रेभ्य इत्यर्थः। तथा नारिभ्यः स्त्रीभ्यश्च अत्तवे अदनाय पर्याप्तं धनम् आ भर आहर। सद्यः तदानीमेव जातः अस्माभिर्दत्तेन सोमेन प्रवृद्धः सन् हे ऋभुष्ठिर । उरुः प्रभूतः स्थिरश्च संग्रामे स तथोक्तः । हे तादृशेंद्र त्वमा भरेति समन्वयः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

With that arrow, bring to us, the praisers, to men, that is, to our sons, and also to women, wealth sufficient for eating, that is, for enjoyment. Having been born at once, that is, having grown by the Soma given by us, O Ṛbhuṣṭhira: vast and steady in battle, he is so called. O Indra, such as you are, bring: this is the construction.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋभुष्ठिर (*ṛbhuṣṭhira*)—O Indra, whose state is steady and great in battles
- सद्यः (*sadyaḥ*)—as soon as we offer Soma
- जातः (*jātaḥ*)—having gained increase
- तेन (*tena*)—with the help of that same arrow
- स्तोतृभ्यः (*stotṛbhyaḥ*)—to us, the praisers
- नृभ्यः (*nṛbhyaḥ*)—to our sons and others
- नारिभ्यः (*nāribhyaḥ*)—to women (wives and others)
- अत्तवे (*attave*)—the wealth needed for enjoyment
- आ भर (*ā bhara*)—procure and give

### Printed p. 168

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, whose state is steady and great in battles, as soon as we offer Soma you, having gained increase, with the help of that same arrow procure the wealth needed for enjoyment for us, the praisers, for our sons and others, and for our wives and others.

##### English translation
Forthwith increased ( by our offerings ), you, mighty and firm, by that (weapon) bring (wealth) for sustenance to us your praisers, our children, and our wives.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒ता च्यौ॒त्नानि॑ ते कृ॒ता वर्षि॑ष्ठानि॒ परी॑णसा ।  
> हृ॒दा वी॒ड्व॑धारयः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एता । च्यौत्नानि । ते । कृता । वर्षिष्ठानि । परीणसा ।  
> हृदा । वीळु । अधारयः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ते त्वया एता एतानि पुरतः सर्वैर्दृश्यमानानि वर्षिष्ठानि अतिशयेन प्रवृद्धानि परीणसा परितो नतानि अत एव च्यौत्नानि इति भावः । भूमेः कीलवद्धारणाय कृता कृतानि । पर्वतास्त्वया कृता इत्यर्थः । या यानि 'हृदा बुद्ध्या वीळु स्थिराणि अधारयः । बुद्ध्या कर्तव्यानीति यान्यधारयः तानीमानीति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, by you these things, seen before us by all, are most greatly grown (varṣiṣṭhāni) and bent on all sides (parīṇasā); and therefore they are cyautna, of firm strength. They are made (kṛtā) to hold the earth like a peg; the meaning is that the mountains were made by you. Whatever things you held firm (vīḷu) with the heart, that is, with the intellect, thinking that they should be made by the intellect, these are they.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऎलै इंद्रने (*èlai iṃdrane*)—(addressed)
- एता (*etā*)—what is seen as known to all
- वर्षिष्ठानि (*varṣiṣṭhāni*)—exceedingly grown
- परीणसा (*parīṇasā*)—spread out and pervading
- च्यौत्नानि (*cyautnāni*)—all that has most excellent strength
- ते (*te*)—by you alone
- कृता (*kṛtā*)—have been made (all these ... )**NOTE**
- हृदा (*hṛdā*)—in your heart alone
- वीळु (*vīḷu*)—firmly
- आधारयः (*ādhārayaḥ*)—you have held

### Printed p. 169

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, all things that are known to all, exceedingly grown, spread out and pervading, and possessed of most excellent strength, have been made by you alone. You hold all these by the resolve of your own mind.

##### English translation
These gignatic far reaching efforts were put forth by you ; you fiixed them firm in your thought.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> विश्वेत्ता विष्णु॒राभ॑रदुरुक्र॒मस्त्वेषि॑तः ।  
> श॒तं म॑हि॒षान्क्षी॑रपा॒कमो॑द॒नं व॑रा॒हमिंद्र॑ एमु॒षम् ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विश्वा । इत् । ता । विष्णुः । आ । अभरत् । उरुऽक्रमः । त्वाऽइषितः ।  
> शतम् । महिषान् । क्षीरऽपाकम् । ओदनम् । वराहम् । इंद्रः । एमुषम् ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्या ऋचो नैरुक्तैतिहासिकमतभेदेन द्विधा योजना । नैरुक्तपक्षे तावत् । हे इंद्र ता तानि यानि त्वया स्रष्टव्यान्युदकानि संति तानि विष्णुः व्यापनशील आदित्यः आभरत् आभरति । लोकाय प्रयच्छतीत्यर्थः । कीदृशो विष्णुः । उरुक्रमः बहुगतिः । किं स्वविरोधेन नेत्याह । त्वेषितः त्वया प्रेरितः । न केवलमुदकान्येव अपि च शतं महिषान् शतसंख्याकान् पशून् । महिषशब्दो गवादेरप्युपलक्षकः । अथवा शतशब्दोऽपरिमितवचनो महिष इति महन्नाम । असंख्यातान् महतो यज्ञान् यजमानेभ्य आभरत् ददातीत्यर्थः । किंच क्षीरपाकं क्षीरपक्वम् ओदनं पायसम् । एतच्चरुपुरोडाशादेरुपलक्षकम् । तद्यजमानेभ्य आभरत् । अथवा सर्वार्थं वृष्टिप्रदानद्वारोदनं प्राहरत् । किंच इंद्रः वराहं जलपूर्णं मेघं हंतीति शेषः । कीदृशं तम् । एमुषम् । आ इत्यस्य स्थाने छांदस एकारः । आमुषमुदकस्य मोषकमित्यर्थः । निरुक्तपक्ष एवम् । ऐतिहासिकपक्षे चरकब्राह्मण इतिहास आम्नायते । विष्णुर्यज्ञः । स देवेभ्य आत्मानमंतरधात् । तमन्यदेवता नाविदन्निंद्रस्त्ववेत् । स इंद्रमब्रवीत्को भवानिति । तमिंद्रः प्रत्यब्रवीदहं दुर्गाणामसुराणां च हंता भवांस्तु क इति । सोऽब्रवीदहं दुर्गादाहर्ता त्वं तु यदि दुर्गाणामसुराणां हंता ततोऽयं वराहो वाममुष एकविंशत्याः पुरां पारेऽश्ममयीनां वसति तस्मिन्नसुराणां वसु वाममस्ति तमिमं जहीति । तस्येंद्रस्ताः पुरो भित्त्वा हृदयमविध्यत्। अधि तत्र यदासीत्तद्विष्णुराहरदिति । सोऽयमितिहासः अस्येदु मातुः सवनेषु ( ऋ. सं. १. ६१. ७ ) ‘ विश्वेत्ता विष्णुः' इत्याभ्यां प्रतिपादितः । तयोर्मध्ये ‘अस्येदु मातुः' इत्यत्र विष्णुना हे इंद्र त्वं दुर्गाणां हंतेत्यात्मानं कथयसि तर्हि वाममुषं वराहमसुरं जहीत्युक्तार्थो ‘ विध्यद्वराहम्' इति पादेन प्रतिपादितः । इंद्रेण च विष्णो त्वं दुर्गादाहर्तेति ब्रूषे मया पुराणि जितान्यसुरश्च घातितस्तस्य वामं वस्वानयेत्युक्तो विष्णुमूर्तिस्तस्य वराहासुरस्य धनं मुमोष । सोऽर्थः ‘मुषायद्विष्णुः पचतम्' इति पादेन सूचितः । स किं पुनर्मुषितवानिति तदत्रोच्यते ' विश्वेत्ता' इति । हे इंद्र त्वेषितस्त्वया प्रेरितो विष्णुर्यज्ञरूपी त्वेषितस्त्वं दुर्गादाहर्ता किल तर्हि त्वं तस्य धनान्याहरेति त्वया प्रेरितः सन्नुरुक्रमो भूत्वा विश्वेत्ता यानि त्वयाहर्तव्यानीत्युक्तानि यानि च तत्र स्थितानि तानि सर्वाण्याभरत् आभरतु । कानि तानीति । शतं महिषानपरिमितान् प्रशस्तान् पदार्थान् तेषां वाहनरूपान् महिषान् वा क्षीरपाकमोदनं च पक्वमात्रमेवौदनं चाभरत् ।' विध्यद्वराहम्' इत्यत्रोक्तोऽर्थश्च चरमपादेनोच्यते । इंद्रस्तु वराहं वराहारं स्वीकृतासुरसर्वस्वं वराहरूपिणं वैमुषमेमुषनामानमथवैमुषं धनानामामोषकं वराहमसुरं हृदयेऽविध्यदिति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Ṛk is explained in two ways, according to the view of the Nairuktas and according to the view of the Aitihāsikas. In the Nairukta view: O Indra, the waters that are to be released by you, those ता (*tā*) the all-pervading Āditya, Viṣṇu, brings (आभरत् (*ābharat*)), that is, gives to the world. Viṣṇu is उरुक्रमः (*urukramaḥ*), of many movements. Does he do it against his own will? No: he is त्वेषितः (*tveṣitaḥ*), urged by you. Not only the waters, but also a hundred महिषान् (*mahiṣān*), that is, a hundred animals; the word mahiṣa stands for cows and the like. Or else the word शतं (*śataṃ*) means an unlimited number and mahiṣa is a name of the great: he gives innumerable great sacrifices to the sacrificers. Further, क्षीरपाकं (*kṣīrapākaṃ*) is boiled in milk, ओदनं (*odanaṃ*) is rice-pudding; this stands for caru, puroḍāśa and the like, and he brings it to the sacrificers. Or else, by giving rain, he brings food for all. And Indra smites the boar (वराहं (*varāhaṃ*)), the cloud full of water. Of what kind? एमुषम् (*emuṣam*): in place of ā there is the Vedic e; it means one who steals the water. This is the Nairukta view.

In the Aitihāsika view, a history is handed down in the Caraka Brāhmaṇa: Viṣṇu is the sacrifice. He hid himself from the gods. The other deities did not find him; Indra alone knew him. Viṣṇu asked Indra, "Who are you?" Indra answered, "I am the slayer of strongholds and of the asuras; who are you?" He said, "I am the bringer out of the stronghold. If you are the slayer of the strongholds of the asuras, then this boar, a stealer of treasure, dwells beyond twenty-one cities of stone; in him is the choice wealth of the asuras; kill him." Indra broke those cities and pierced the boar in the heart; and whatever was there, Viṣṇu brought. This history is set forth in the two Ṛks beginning अस्येदु मातुः सवनेषु (*asyedu mātuḥ savaneṣu*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1. 61. 7) and विश्वेत्ता विष्णुः (*viśvettā viṣṇuḥ*). Of the two, in अस्येदु मातुः (*asyedu mātuḥ*) the sense "O Indra, you say you are the slayer of strongholds; then kill the boar-asura, the stealer of treasure" is conveyed by the pāda विध्यद्वराहम् (*vidhyadvarāham*). And the sense "O Viṣṇu, you say you are the bringer out of the stronghold; I have conquered the cities and slain the asura; bring his wealth", spoken by Indra, after which Viṣṇu, in the form of the sacrifice, stole the wealth of that boar-asura, is indicated by the pāda मुषायद्विष्णुः पचतम् (*muṣāyadviṣṇuḥ pacatam*). What then did he steal? This is told here by विश्वेत्ता (*viśvettā*): O Indra, Viṣṇu in the form of the sacrifice, urged by you, who are the bringer out of strongholds, to bring the wealth of that asura, became उरुक्रमः (*urukramaḥ*) and brought all that stood there. What is that? A hundred महिषान् (*mahiṣān*), that is, countless excellent things, or buffaloes as their carriers, and rice boiled in milk, merely cooked rice. The sense stated in विध्यद्वराहम् (*vidhyadvarāham*) is told by the last pāda: Indra pierced in the heart the boar, the taker of all the asura's possessions, in the form of a boar, named Vaimuṣa or Emuṣa, or else the stealer of treasures.

### Printed p. 170

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उरुक्रमः (*urukramaḥ*)—(O Indra) one of wide movement
- त्वेषितः (*tveṣitaḥ*)—one urged by you alone
- विष्णुः (*viṣṇuḥ*)—the pervading Āditya
- ता विश्वा इत् (*tā viśvā it*)—all the waters created by you
- आ भरत् (*ā bharat*)—bears
- शतं (*śataṃ*)—numbering a hundred
- महिषान् (*mahiṣān*)—animals
- क्षीरपाकं (*kṣīrapākaṃ*)—cooked with milk
- ओदनं (*odanaṃ*)—rice, he himself bears also
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- एमुषं (*emuṣaṃ*)—holding the water (stealing)
- वराहं (*varāhaṃ*)—cleaves the cloud

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, the all-pervading Āditya, of wide movement and urged by you alone, bears all the waters created by you. He bears a hundred animals and rice cooked with milk. Indra cleaves the cloud that is full of water.

### Printed p. 171

##### English translation
The wide-traversing Sun, depatched by you, brings (to the world) all these ( waters which you creat ); he brings hundreds of cattle and rice boiled in milk ; it is Indra who slays the water-stealing boar.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

There are two kinds of explanation of this Ṛk: one is the Nairukta view, the other the Aitihāsika view. Besides these two kinds of meaning, Yāska also states an interpretation (nirvacana) of this Ṛk. It is necessary to explain all these here in order.

**Explanation in the Nairukta view**

(हे इंद्र (*he iṃdra*)—O Indra). उरुक्रमः (*urukramaḥ*)—of manifold movement, that is, constantly moving; विष्णुः (*viṣṇuḥ*)—Āditya, who pervades everywhere with his rays; त्वेषितः (*tveṣitaḥ*)—urged by you; ता (*tā*)—the waters created by you; आ भरत् (*ā bharat*)—bears for the benefit of the world, that is, gives. (And) शतं महिषान् (*śataṃ mahiṣān*)—a hundred animals, or many animals; (आ भरत् (*ā bharat*))—he gives to the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice; (and) क्षीरपाकं (*kṣīrapākaṃ*)—cooked with milk; ओदनं (*odanaṃ*)—rice-pudding, that is, oblations in the form of caru, puroḍāśa and the like; (आ भरत् (*ā bharat*))—he gives to the sacrificer. [Or ओदनं (*odanaṃ*)—by pouring rain on the earth he brings forth plants and the like, becomes the cause of the production of food and other nourishment, and gives them to the sacrificers]. (And) इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra; एमुषं (*emuṣaṃ*)—that which holds back the water and does not let it pour on the earth as rain; वराहं (*varāhaṃ*)—the cloud full of water, he strikes.

O Indra, Āditya, who is always moving, draws with his rays the water in oceans and other reservoirs, causes that water to be changed into clouds, and pours rain on the earth for the benefit of the world. This work done by Āditya is impelled by you (Indra); that is, it is by Indra's command that Āditya pours rain on the earth for the benefit of the world. Besides this, for the use of the sacrificers who perform sacrifices, he gives innumerable animals fit for sacrifice, and rice and other food cooked in milk, for the purpose of oblation. All these works Āditya does only when impelled by Indra. Having made Āditya do these works, Indra himself strikes the clouds, which, though full of water, hold it back so that it does not fall on the earth as rain, and makes the rain fall. All this is the power of Indra.

### Printed p. 172

All the explanation given above belongs to the Nairukta view. Now let us consider what the explanation in the Aitihāsika view is. In this matter a history is stated in the Caraka Brāhmaṇa:

विष्णुर्यज्ञः । स देवेभ्य आत्मानमुतरधात् । तमस्यदेवता साविंदन्निंद्रस्त्ववेत् । स इंद्रमब्रवीत्को भवानिति । तमिंद्रः प्रत्यब्रवीदहं दुर्गाणामसुराणां च हंता भवांस्त्व क इति । सोऽब्रवीदहं दुर्गादाहर्ता त्वं तु यदि दुर्गाणामसुराणां हंता ततोऽयं वराहो वाममुष एकविंशत्याः पुरां पारेऽश्ममयीनां वसति तस्मिन्नसुराणां वसु वाममस्ति तमिमं जहीति । तस्येंद्रस्त्राः पुरो भित्त्व हृदयमविध्यत् । अधि तत्र यदासीत्तद्विष्णुराहरदिति । सोऽयमितिहासः ॥ (*viṣṇuryajñaḥ | sa devebhya ātmānamutaradhāt | tamasyadevatā sāviṃdanniṃdrastvavet | sa iṃdramabravītko bhavāniti | tamiṃdraḥ pratyabravīdahaṃ durgāṇāmasurāṇāṃ ca haṃtā bhavāṃstva ka iti | so 'bravīdahaṃ durgādāhartā tvaṃ tu yadi durgāṇāmasurāṇāṃ haṃtā tato 'yaṃ varāho vāmamuṣa ekaviṃśatyāḥ purāṃ pāre 'śmamayīnāṃ vasati tasminnasurāṇāṃ vasu vāmamasti tamimaṃ jahīti | tasyeṃdrastrāḥ puro bhittva hṛdayamavidhyat | adhi tatra yadāsīttadviṣṇurāharaditi | so 'yamitihāsaḥ ‖*)

The main purport of this is as follows. Viṣṇu is the sacrifice. That Viṣṇu, in the form of the sacrifice, once left the gods and went away and stayed in a secret place. The other deities did not come to know this; only Indra knew. Then, when Indra went to the place where Viṣṇu was, Viṣṇu asked Indra who he was. In reply Indra said, "I am the one who destroys strongholds and asuras. But who are you?" Viṣṇu said, "I am the one who carries off from strongholds the wealth and so on belonging to the asuras. Who are you? What work do you do?" When he asked again, Viṣṇu said thus: "If you are, as you say, the destroyer of the strongholds and of the wealth of the asuras, then the asura named Varāha, who has carried off wealth and hidden it in himself, lives in a fortress firmly built of stones at the end of twenty-one cities (forts) from here. In him there is much wealth of the asuras. Therefore kill that asura." Hearing this, Indra destroyed those cities of the asuras and pierced the heart of the asura called Varāha and killed him. Then Viṣṇu brought from there all the wealth that was in the asura. This is the main purport of this history. This matter is also stated in the present Ṛk, and in the Ṛk

अस्येदु मातुः सवनेषु सद्यो महः पितुं पपिवा‌ञ्चार्वन्ना । मुषायद्विष्णुः पचतं सहीयान्विध्यद्वराहं तिरो अद्रिमस्ता ॥ (*asyedu mātuḥ savaneṣu sadyo mahaḥ pituṃ papivāñcārvannā | muṣāyadviṣṇuḥ pacataṃ sahīyānvidhyadvarāhaṃ tiro adrimastā ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1–61–7)

and the commentator, saying so, has explained by fitting together the words found in the present Ṛk and in the quoted one with the history stated above:

अस्येदु मातुः इत्यत्र विष्णुना हे इंद्र त्वं दुर्गाणां हंतेत्यात्मानं कथयसि तर्हि वाममुषं वराहमसुरं जहीत्युक्तार्थो विध्यद्वराहं इति पादेन प्रतिपादितः । (*asyedu mātuḥ ityatra viṣṇunā he iṃdra tvaṃ durgāṇāṃ haṃtetyātmānaṃ kathayasi tarhi vāmamuṣaṃ varāhamasuraṃ jahītyuktārtho vidhyadvarāhaṃ iti pādena pratipāditaḥ |*)

### Printed p. 173

The meaning of the words of Viṣṇu stated in the history above, "O Indra, you say that you are the destroyer of strongholds and of the asuras. If so, kill the asura named Varāha, who has carried off wealth and the like and hidden it in himself", is stated in the last pāda विध्यद्वराहं तिरो अद्रिमस्ता (*vidhyadvarāhaṃ tiro adrimastā*) of the quoted Ṛk अस्येदु मातुः (*asyedu mātuḥ*).

इंद्रेण च विष्णो त्वं दुर्गादाहर्तेति ब्रूषे मया पुराणि जितास्यसुरश्च घातितस्तस्य वामं वस्वानयेत्युक्तो विष्णुमूर्तिस्तस्य वराहासुरस्य धनं मुमोष । सोऽर्थः मुषायद्विष्णुः पचतं सहीयान् इति पादेन सूचितः ॥ (*iṃdreṇa ca viṣṇo tvaṃ durgādāharteti brūṣe mayā purāṇi jitāsyasuraśca ghātitastasya vāmaṃ vasvānayetyukto viṣṇumūrtistasya varāhāsurasya dhanaṃ mumoṣa | so 'rthaḥ muṣāyadviṣṇuḥ pacataṃ sahīyān iti pādena sūcitaḥ ‖*)

And Indra said to Viṣṇu: "O Viṣṇu, you say that you are the one who brings wealth and so on out of the strongholds of the asuras. Now I have conquered many enemy cities and killed the asura. Go and bring the wealth that is in that asura." Viṣṇu agreed to this and carried off and brought the wealth that was in that boar-asura. This detail is made known by the pāda मुषायद्विष्णुः पचतं सहीयान् (*muṣāyadviṣṇuḥ pacataṃ sahīyān*) of the quoted Ṛk.

स किं पुनर्मुषितवानिति तदक्रोच्यते । विश्वेत्ता इति । हे इंद्र त्वेषितस्त्वया प्रेरितो विष्णुर्यज्ञरूपिः त्वेषितस्त्वं दुर्गादाहर्ता किल तर्हि त्वं तस्य धनान्याहरेति त्वया प्रेरितः सन्नुरुक्रमो भूत्वा विश्वेत्ता यानि च तत्र स्थितानि तानि सर्वाण्याभरत् । आभरतु । कानि तानीति । शतं महिषानपरिमितान् प्रशस्तान् पदार्थान् तेषां वाहनरूपान् महिषान्वा क्षीरपाकमोदनं च पक्वमात्रमेवौदनं चाभरत् ॥ (*sa kiṃ punarmuṣitavāniti tadakrocyate | viśvettā iti | he iṃdra tveṣitastvayā prerito viṣṇuryajñarūpiḥ tveṣitastvaṃ durgādāhartā kila tarhi tvaṃ tasya dhanānyāhareti tvayā preritaḥ sannurukramo bhūtvā viśvettā yāni ca tatra sthitāni tāni sarvāṇyābharat | ābharatu | kāni tānīti | śataṃ mahiṣānaparimitān praśastān padārthān teṣāṃ vāhanarūpān mahiṣānvā kṣīrapākamodanaṃ ca pakvamātramevaudanaṃ cābharat ‖*)

It is said that Viṣṇu again brought many more things, carrying them off. Its detail is stated clearly in the present Ṛk विश्वेत्ता (*viśvettā*): O Indra, as soon as Viṣṇu, the sacrifice-formed, was urged by you with the words, "O Viṣṇu, you say that you are the one who carries off wealth and so on from the strongholds of the asuras; then bring the wealth and so on that is in this boar-asura", he agreed, saying "So be it", went swiftly to the city of the asura, and carried off and brought all the wealth and so on that was in the asura, that is, whatever things Indra had told him to bring. What are those things Viṣṇu brought? The most excellent and innumerable things, wealth and other choice things, and the animals such as buffaloes that carry them, and food and other nourishment cooked with milk and the like: he carried off and brought all of it as Indra had said. This detail is clearly indicated in the present Ṛk.

### Printed p. 174

विध्यद्वराहं इत्यत्रोक्तोऽर्थश्च चरमपादेनोच्यते । इंद्रस्तु वराहारं स्वीकृतासुरसर्वस्वं वराहरूपिणं वैमुषमेमुषनामानमथवैमुषं धनानामामोषकं वराहमसुरं हृदयेऽविध्यदिति (*vidhyadvarāhaṃ ityatrokto 'rthaśca caramapādenocyate | iṃdrastu varāhāraṃ svīkṛtāsurasarvasvaṃ varāharūpiṇaṃ vaimuṣamemuṣanāmānamathavaimuṣaṃ dhanānāmāmoṣakaṃ varāhamasuraṃ hṛdaye 'vidhyaditi*)

Now they explain the meaning of the last pāda of the present Ṛk, विध्यद्वराहमिंद्र एमुषं (*vidhyadvarāhamiṃdra emuṣaṃ*). This asura called Varāha was in the form of a varāha (boar) and had carried off and stored much wealth from many. Therefore it is customary to call him एमुषं (*emuṣaṃ*), that is, धनानामामोषकं (*dhanānāmāmoṣakaṃ*), one who steals wealth. Indra, by his strength and power, pierced the chest of this asura named Emuṣa, in the form of a boar, and killed him: this is the sense set out in the last pāda of the present Ṛk.

In the Taittirīya Saṃhitā of the Kṛṣṇa Yajurveda also, the account of this matter is stated in the same way:

यज्ञो देवेभ्यो निलायत विष्णूरूपं कृत्वा स पृथिवीं प्राविशत्तं देवा हस्तान्सग्‌ं रभ्यैच्छन्तमिंद्र उपर्युपर्यत्यक्रामत्तोऽब्रवीत्को मायमुपर्युपर्यतिक्रमीत्यहं दुर्गे हंतेत्यथ कस्त्वमित्यहं दुर्गादाहर्तेति सोऽब्रवीद्गिरे वै हंतास्योऽथा वराहोऽयं वाम-मोषः सप्तानां गिरीणां परस्ताद्वित्तं वेद्यमसुराणां बिभर्ति तं जहि यदि दुर्गे हंतासीति सदर्भप्रंजिलमुद्गृह्य सप्तगिरीन् भित्त्वा तमहन्तोऽब्रविद्विष्णुर्गाप्ता आहर्ता वो ऽथा एतमाहरेति तमेभ्यो यज्ञ एव यज्ञमाहरद्यत्तद्वित्तं वेद्यमसुराणामविंदंत तदेकु वेद्यै वेदित्वं ॥ (*yajño devebhyo nilāyata viṣṇūrūpaṃ kṛtvā sa pṛthivīṃ prāviśattaṃ devā hastānsagṃ rabhyaicchantamiṃdra uparyuparyatyakrāmatto 'bravītko māyamuparyuparyatikramītyahaṃ durge haṃtetyatha kastvamityahaṃ durgādāharteti so 'bravīdgire vai haṃtāsyo 'thā varāho 'yaṃ vāma-moṣaḥ saptānāṃ girīṇāṃ parastādvittaṃ vedyamasurāṇāṃ bibharti taṃ jahi yadi durge haṃtāsīti sadarbhapraṃjilamudgṛhya saptagirīn bhittvā tamahanto 'bravidviṣṇurgāptā āhartā vo 'thā etamāhareti tamebhyo yajña eva yajñamāharadyattadvittaṃ vedyamasurāṇāmaviṃdaṃta tadeku vedyai veditvaṃ ‖*)

(Trai. Saṃ. 6-2-4-2, 3)**NOTE**

To explain how the sacrificial altar came to have the name vedi, a history is stated in the Yajurveda:

Formerly, for some reason, the sacrifice took the form of Viṣṇu, left the gods and went away. Then that Viṣṇu, the sacrifice-formed, entered the earth and remained hidden so that no one knew. The gods, not seeing him, held one another by the hand and searched everywhere, yet they did not find the place where Viṣṇu was. But when Indra passed over the region where Viṣṇu was hidden, Viṣṇu below said, "Who is it? Who passes over the place where I am?" Hearing that, Indra said, "I am the one who strikes and destroys the strongholds of the asuras, who

### Printed p. 175

a hero: that am I. But who are you?" To this Viṣṇu gave the reply: "I am the one who brings the wealth and other things that are there, after the strongholds and the like of the asuras have been destroyed." And addressing Indra, he said: "O man, if you are indeed the one who destroys the enemy cities as you claim, then beyond seven mountains from here there lives an asura named Varāha. He has carried off the wealth and other possessions of the asuras and keeps them inside his strong fortress. If you have the power, as you say, to destroy the strongholds of the asuras, strike down and destroy that asura Varāha. Only then will your power be known." Hearing this, Indra took a bunch of darbha grass that was near, made that very thing his weapon, split the seven unbreakable mountains, and struck and killed that Varāha-asura. Then, addressing Viṣṇu, he said: "O man, as you said, I have split the seven mountains and killed the asura who was there. By this my strength and power have become clear. You said that you are the one who brings the wealth and so on that is in the strongholds of the asuras that have been destroyed. Now, as you said, go and bring the wealth and other things that are there; then your power will be shown." When he said this, Viṣṇu agreed, and in the very form of the sacrifice he carried off the wealth and other things that were in that asura and brought them to the gods. Because in this way the gods obtained the wealth of the asuras through the sacrifice, the place where sacrifice is performed came to be called vedi: this is the main purport of this history.

In order to explain the meaning of the word वराहः (*varāhaḥ*), Yāska has given an interpretation in this way:

वराहः । वराहो मेघो भवति वराहारः । वरमाहारमाहार्षीः इति च ब्राह्मणं । विष्यस्वराहं तिरो अद्रिमस्ता इत्यपि निगमो भवति । आयमपि इतरो वराह एतस्मादेव । बृहति मूलानि वरं वरं मूलं बृहतीति वा । वराहमिंद्र एमुषं इत्यपि निगमो भवति ॥ (*varāhaḥ | varāho megho bhavati varāhāraḥ | varamāhāramāhārṣīḥ iti ca brāhmaṇaṃ | viṣyasvarāhaṃ tiro adrimastā ityapi nigamo bhavati | āyamapi itaro varāha etasmādeva | bṛhati mūlāni varaṃ varaṃ mūlaṃ bṛhatīti vā | varāhamiṃdra emuṣaṃ ityapi nigamo bhavati ‖*)

(Ni. 5-4)

Varāha means a cloud. Because this cloud, by pouring rain, provides excellent food, the cloud has come to have the name varāhāra or varāha. वरमाहारमाहार्षीः (*varamāhāramāhārṣīḥ*) is the Brāhmaṇa sentence that supports the meaning in this way. विद्यद् वराहं तिरो अद्रिमस्ता (*vidyad varāhaṃ tiro adrimastā*) is also an example of another Vedic sentence. In this the sense is indicated that Indra hurled his thunderbolt from afar and split the cloud. That is, in this example the meaning "cloud" of the word varāha is clear. The word varāha has another meaning too, "boar". This word (the word varāha) is also derived from the same root. Because it tears up with its tusks the roots of plants, that is, of vegetation and so on, and because it uproots and destroys those excellent tubers and roots together with their base, the pig or boar has the name varāha. In this matter the Vedic sentence in the present Ṛk, वराहमिंद्र एमुषं (*varāhamiṃdra emuṣaṃ*), "Indra split and killed the boar (the asura) that destroys food", serves as the example supporting this kind of meaning. This is the main purport of the Nirukta.

### Printed p. 176

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तु॒वि॒क्षं ते॒ सुकृ॑तं सू॒मयं॒ धनुः॑ सा॒धुर्बुं॒दो हि॑र॒ण्ययः॑ ।  
> उ॒भा ते॑ बा॒हू रण्या॒ सुसं॑स्कृत ऋदू॒पे चि॑दृदू॒वृधा॑ ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तुविऽक्षम् । ते । सुऽकृतम् । सुऽमयम् । धनुः । साधुः । बुंदः । हिरण्ययः ।  
> उभा । ते । बाहू इति । रण्या । सुऽसंस्कृता । ऋदुऽपे । चित् । ऋदुऽवृधा ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ‘एषा निरुक्त एकमपि पदं (अ)विहाय यास्केन व्याख्याता । तदेव लिख्यते- तुविक्षं बहुविक्षेपं महाविक्षेपं वा ते सुकृतं सूमयं सुसुखं धनुः साधयिता ते बुंदो हिरण्मयः । उभौ ते बाहू रण्यौ रमणीयौ सांग्राम्यौ वर्दूपे अर्दनपातिनौ गमनपातिनो मर्मण्यर्दनवेधिनौ गमनवेधिनौ वा ' (निरु. ६. ३३ ) इति ॥ ॥ ३० ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Yāska has explained this Ṛk in the Nirukta without leaving out a single word, and the same is written here (निरु. ६. ३३ (*niru. 6. 33*)). तुविक्षं (*tuvikṣaṃ*) is "of many casts" or "of great casting". Your सुकृतं (*sukṛtaṃ*), well made, सूमयं (*sūmayaṃ*), very comfortable, धनुः (*dhanuḥ*) bow; your बुंदः (*buṃdaḥ*) is the accomplisher, golden. Both your arms are रण्यौ (*raṇyau*), delightful, belonging to battle; ऋदूपे (*ṛdūpe*), falling so as to crush, falling so as to move; piercing a vital spot by crushing, or piercing by moving.**NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ते (*te*)—(O Indra) your
- धनुः (*dhanuḥ*)—bow
- तुविक्षं (*tuvikṣaṃ*)—that which strikes a far-distant target
- सुकृतं (*sukṛtaṃ*)—that which is well made
- सुमयं (*sumayaṃ*)—that which is endowed with beauty

### Printed p. 177

- साधुः (*sādhuḥ*)—excellent
- बुंदः (*buṃdaḥ*)—arrow
- हिरण्ययः (*hiraṇyayaḥ*)—made of gold
- ते (*te*)—your
- उभा बाहू (*ubhā bāhū*)—both arms also
- रण्या (*raṇyā*)—delightful
- संसंस्कृता (*saṃsaṃskṛtā*)—of excellent make
- ऋदुपे चित् (*ṛdupe cit*)—possessed of the power to hurl enemies down
- ऋदुवृधा (*ṛduvṛdhā*)—possessed of the power to hurl down and destroy

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, your bow strikes a far-distant target, is well made and endowed with beauty, and your arrow is made of gold. Both your arms also are delightful, of excellent make, possessed of the power to hurl enemies down, and possessed of the power to destroy.

##### English translation
Far-darting is your well-made auspicious bow, unfailing is your golden arrow ; your two warlike arms are ready equipped, destructively overthrowing, destructively piercing.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In explaining the word बुंद (*buṃda*), Yāska took the present Ṛk itself as the example and stated an interpretation for this Ṛk. We have explained the meaning of the word bunda, as Yāska states it, in the Special Topics of the sixth Ṛk of this sūkta (page 166). Bunda means arrow. The interpretation of the present Ṛk is as follows:

तुविक्षं बहुविक्षेपं महाविक्षेपं वा । ते सुकृतं सूमयं सुसुखं धनुः साधयिता । ते बुंदो हिरण्यय उभौ ते बाहू रण्या रमणीयौ सांग्राम्यौ वा । ऋदूपे अर्दनपातिनौ गमनपातिनौ शब्दपातिनौ दूरपातिनौ वा । (*tuvikṣaṃ bahuvikṣepaṃ mahāvikṣepaṃ vā | te sukṛtaṃ sūmayaṃ susukhaṃ dhanuḥ sādhayitā | te buṃdo hiraṇyaya ubhau te bāhū raṇyā ramaṇīyau sāṃgrāmyau vā | ṛdūpe ardanapātinau gamanapātinau śabdapātinau dūrapātinau vā |*) मर्मर्ण्यर्दनवेधिनौ (*marmarṇyardanavedhinau*)**NOTE** गमनवेधिनौ शब्दवेधिनौ दूरवेधिनौ वा (*gamanavedhinau śabdavedhinau dūravedhinau vā*)

(Ni. 6-34)**NOTE**

O Indra, your arrow is of great power, able to travel very far, or it goes forth discharged with great strength. Your bow is well made, of good shape, and gives delight. Your arrow is made of gold. Both your arms are beautiful and exceedingly strong for fighting. To the word ऋदूपे (*ṛdūpe*) used in this Ṛk (the arrow) Yāska has explained many kinds of meaning: that which strikes the target with its swift motion and brings it down, or brings it down by its twang; or that which, even if the object is far away, approaches it and strikes it down; or that which splits the vital parts (Vital or valnerable parts) of the target by its swift movement and by its sharp point; or that which, by its terrible sound or by its swift movement, pierces and splits an object that is far away.

### Printed p. 178

## Sūkta 78 (Maṇḍala 8)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ‘ पुरोळाशं नः' इति दशर्चं नवमं सूक्तं काण्वस्य कुरुसुतेरार्षम् । आद्या नव गायत्र्यो दशमी बृहती । इंद्रो देवता । तथा चानुक्रांतं-' पुरोळाशं दश बृहत्यंतम्' इति । सूक्तविनियोगो लैंगिकः । महाव्रते निष्केवल्ये गायत्रतृचाशीतावाद्यास्तिस्र ऋचः । तथा च सूत्रितं - ' पुरोळाशं नो अंधस इति तिस्रः ' ( ऐ. आ. ५. २. ३ ) इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta पुरोळाशं नः (*puroḷāśaṃ naḥ*) has ten Ṛks; it is the ninth sūkta, and its seer is Kurusuti of the Kaṇva line (काण्वस्य कुरुसुतेरार्षम् (*kāṇvasya kurusuterārṣam*)). The first nine Ṛks are in the Gāyatrī metre and the tenth is a Bṛhatī. The deity is Indra. As the Anukramaṇī says: पुरोळाशं दश बृहत्यंतम् (*puroḷāśaṃ daśa bṛhatyaṃtam*). The use of the sūkta is by indication (लैंगिकः (*laiṃgikaḥ*)). In the Mahāvrata, in the Niṣkevalya recitation, the first three Ṛks serve as the Gāyatra triplet. So it is stated by the sūtra: पुरोळाशं नो अंधस इति तिस्रः (*puroḷāśaṃ no aṃdhasa iti tisraḥ*) (Ai. Ā. 5. 2. 3).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, पुरोळाशं नः (*puroḷāśaṃ naḥ*), is the ninth**NOTE** sūkta in the eighth Anuvāka. It has ten Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Kurusutiḥṇvaḥ**NOTE**; the deity is Indra. The first nine Ṛks of this sūkta are in the Gāyatrī metre; the tenth is in the Bṛhatī metre. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: पुरोळाशं दश बृहत्कांतं (*puroḷāśaṃ daśa bṛhatkāṃtaṃ*). The use of the sūkta is by indication. In the Mahāvrata sacrifice, in the Niṣkevalya recitation, the first three Ṛks of this sūkta are used for the Ṛks in the Gāyatrī metre; this is specified in the Aitareya Āraṇyaka, in the fifth Āraṇyaka, by the sentence पुरोळाशं नो अंधस इति तिस्रः (*puroḷāśaṃ no aṃdhasa iti tisraḥ*) (Ai. Ā. 5-3-6).**NOTE**

##### Particulars

- Sūkta—78**NOTE**
- Maṇḍala—8; Anuvāka—8; Sūkta—78**NOTE**
- Aṣṭaka—6; Adhyāya—5; Varga—30, 31
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta—10
- Ṛṣi—Kurusutiḥṇvaḥ
- Devatā—Indra
- Chandas—1-9 Gāyatrī; 10 Bṛhatī

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पु॒रो॒ळाशं॑ नो॒ अंध॑स॒ इंद्र॑ स॒हस्र॒मा भ॑र ।  
> श॒ता च॑ शूर॒ गोना॑म् ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 179

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पुरोळाशम् । नः । अंधसः । इंद्र । सहस्रम् । आ । भर ।  
> शता । च । शूर । गोनाम् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे शूर इंद्र पुरोळाशं पुरो दीयमानमेतत्संज्ञकम् अंधसः अन्नं स्वीकृत्य गोनां गवां सहस्रं शता शतानि च नः अस्मभ्यम् आ भर आहर। अथवा नोऽस्मभ्यं पुरतो दीयमानमंधसोऽंधो व्यंजनं सहस्रं सहस्रसंख्याकं गोसहस्रं चाहरेति योज्यम्

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O hero Indra, accepting the पुरोळाशं (*puroḷāśaṃ*), the food of that name which is offered in front, bring to us a thousand and hundreds of cows (गोनां (*gonāṃ*) means of cows). Or else it is to be construed thus: bring to us the food offered before us, the condiment, and a thousand cows.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शूर (*śūra*)—O mighty one
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- पुरोळाशं (*puroḷāśaṃ*)—called puroḍāśa
- अंधसः (*aṃdhasaḥ*)—food (accepting)
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- गोनां (*gonāṃ*)—of cows
- सहस्रं (*sahasraṃ*)—the number of a thousand
- शता च (*śatā ca*)—and the number of a hundred
- आ भर (*ā bhara*)—bring

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O mighty Indra, accepting the food called puroḍāśa, bring us cows in thousands and in hundreds.

##### English translation
(Accepting) our offering of sacrificial viands, O hero Indra, bring us thousands and hundreds of cows.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ नो॑ भर॒ व्यंज॑नं॒ गामश्व॑म॒भ्यंज॑नम् ।  
> सचा॑ म॒ना हि॑र॒ण्यया॑ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । नः । भर । विऽअंजनम् । गाम् । अश्वम् । अभिऽअंजनम् ।  
> सचा । मना । हिरण्यया ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 180

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वं नः अस्मभ्यं व्यंजनं गामश्वमभ्यंजनं तैलं च आ भर। मना मननीयानि हिरण्यया हिरण्मयान्युपकरणानि सचा सहाभरेति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, bring to us the condiment, a cow, a horse and the अभ्यंजनं (*abhyaṃjanaṃ*), that is, oil. मना (*manā*) means desirable, हिरण्यया (*hiraṇyayā*) means golden implements: bring these together with them (सचा (*sacā*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नः (*naḥ*)—(O Indra) to us
- व्यंजनं (*vyaṃjanaṃ*)—condiment substances
- गां (*gāṃ*)—cows
- अश्वं (*aśvaṃ*)—horses
- अभ्यंजनं (*abhyaṃjanaṃ*)—oil and the like
- मना (*manā*)—valuable
- हिरण्यया (*hiraṇyayā*)—golden things
- सचा (*sacā*)—together
- आ भर (*ā bhara*)—bring

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, bring us together condiment substances, cows, horses, oil and the like, and valuable gold.

##### English translation
Bring us condiments, cows, horses, and oil, (bring us) with them precious golden (vessels).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒त नः॑ कर्ण॒शोभ॑ना पु॒रूणि॑ धृष्ण॒वा भ॑र ।  
> त्वं हि शृ॑ण्वि॒षे व॑सो ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । नः । कर्णऽशोभना । पुरूणि । धृष्णो इति । आ । भर ।  
> त्वम् । हि । शृण्विषे । वसो इति ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उत अपि च नः अस्मभ्यं कर्णशोभना कर्णाभरणानि पुरूणि बहूनि आ भर । हे धृष्णो धर्षकेंद्र वसो वासयितरिंद्र त्वं हि खलु शृण्विषे श्रूयसे । किमिति । उदारोऽयमिंद्र इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

And also bring to us many ear-ornaments (कर्णशोभना (*karṇaśobhanā*) means ornaments of the ear; पुरूणि (*purūṇi*) means many). O bold Indra (धृष्णो (*dhṛṣṇo*), the daring one), O वसो (*vaso*), the one who causes to dwell: you indeed are heard of (शृण्विषे (*śṛṇviṣe*), you are heard). How? As this Indra is generous.

### Printed p. 181

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उतॆ (*utè*)—and
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- कर्णशोभना (*karṇaśobhanā*)—that serve as ornaments for the ear
- पुरूणि (*purūṇi*)—many ornaments
- आ भर (*ā bhara*)—bring and provide (O Indra)
- धृष्णो (*dhṛṣṇo*)—O Indra, destroyer of enemies
- वसो (*vaso*)—giver of dwellings
- त्वं हि (*tvaṃ hi*)—you alone
- शृण्विषे (*śṛṇviṣe*)—you are renowned

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, bring and provide us with many ornaments that serve as adornment for our ears. O Indra, destroyer of enemies and giver of dwellings, you alone are renowned.

##### English translation
O resolute one, bring us many ear-ornaments; giver of dwellings, you are renowned.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नकीं॑ वृधी॒क इं॑द्र ते॒ न सु॒षा न सु॒दा उ॒त ।  
> नान्यस्त्वच्छू॑र वा॒घतः॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नकीम् । वृधीकः । इंद्र । ते । न । सुऽसाः । न । सुऽदाः । उत ।  
> न । अन्यः । त्वत् । शूर । वाघतः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ते त्वत्तोऽन्यः कश्चित् वृधीकः वर्धयिता नकीं नैव । तथा सुषाः सुष्ठु संभक्ता संग्रामादौ त्वत्तोऽन्यः न । उत अपि च सुदाः सुदाता न । तथा हे शूर त्वत् त्वत्तोऽन्यः वाघतः । ऋत्विग्नामैतत् । ऋत्विजो यजमानस्य नेता नान्यः अस्ति त्वामृते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, no one other than you is a promoter of growth (वृधीकः (*vṛdhīkaḥ*), one who makes grow); there is none at all. Likewise no one other than you is a good sharer (सुषाः (*suṣāḥ*), one who shares well) in battle and the like. And no one is a good giver (सुदाः (*sudāḥ*), a good donor). And, O hero, no one other than you is a वाघतः (*vāghataḥ*); this is a name of the priest (ṛtvij). There is no leader of the priest and the sacrificer other than you.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- ते (*te*)—other than you
- वृधीकः (*vṛdhīkaḥ*)—promoter of growth
- नकिः (*nakiḥ*)—there is no one else at all
- सुषाः (*suṣāḥ*)—those who carry off the enemy's wealth in battles and the like
- न (*na*)—there is no other than you

### Printed p. 182

- उतॆ (*utè*)—and
- सुदाः (*sudāḥ*)—excellent maidservants like you **NOTE**
- न (*na*)—there is no one else
- शूर (*śūra*)—O valiant Indra
- त्वत् (*tvat*)—better than you
- वाघतः (*vāghataḥ*)—leaders of the sacrifice (priests)
- न अन्यः (*na anyaḥ*)—there is no other

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, there is no promoter of growth other than you. There is no one other than you who carries off the enemy's wealth in battles and the like. There are no excellent maidservants like you. O valiant Indra, there are no priests better than you either.

##### English translation
There is no prosperer other than you, no divider of the spoil, no giver of boons; O hero, there is no (leader) of the sacrificer other than you

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नकी॒मिंद्रो॒ निक॑र्तवे॒ न श॒क्रः परि॑शक्तवे ।  
> विश्वं॑ शृणोति॒ पश्य॑ति ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नकीम् । इंद्रः । निऽकर्तवे । न । शक्रः । परिऽशक्तवे ।  
> विश्वम् । शृणोति । पश्यति ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयम् इंद्रः निकर्तवे निकर्तुं नकीं नैव शक्यः । तथा शक्रः शक्तोऽयं परिशक्तवे परिभावाय न शक्यत इति । स तु विश्वं शृणोति पश्यति च ॥ ॥ ३१ ॥ स मन्यु मनामदब्धो नि चिकीषते । पुरा निदर्थिकीषते ॥ ६ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Indra cannot be brought down (निकर्तवे (*nikartave*), to be brought down); there is none at all who can do it. Likewise this Indra, being mighty (शक्रः (*śakraḥ*)), cannot be overpowered (परिशक्तवे (*pariśaktave*), for overpowering). He hears and sees everything.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- निकर्तवे (*nikartave*)—to be brought down from his own place
- नकीं (*nakīṃ*)—it is not possible for anyone
- शक्रः (*śakraḥ*)—Indra, who is all-powerful
- परिशक्तवे (*pariśaktave*)—to be defeated
- न (*na*)—it is not possible (that same Indra)
- विश्वं (*viśvaṃ*)—everything
- शृणोति (*śṛṇoti*)—hears
- पश्यति (*paśyati*)—sees

### Printed p. 183

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

It is not possible for anyone to bring Indra down from his own place. It is not possible for anyone to defeat the all-powerful Indra. That same Indra hears and sees everything.

##### English translation
Indra cannot be brought low, he cannot be overpowered—he hears, he sees all.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स म॒न्युं मर्त्या॑ना॒मद॑ब्धो॒ नि चि॑कीषते ।  
> पु॒रा नि॒दश्चि॑कीषते ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । मन्युम् । मर्त्यानाम् । अदब्धः । नि । चिकीषते ।  
> पुरा । निदः । चिकीषते ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सः इंद्रः मन्युं क्रोधम् । केषाम् । मर्त्यानाम् । अदब्धः केनाप्यहिंसितः सन् नि चिकीषते निकरोति । किं मन्यु प्राप्यैव नेत्याह । निदः निंदायाः पुरा पूर्वमेव चिकीषते । यदा तं निंदितुमिच्छति कश्चित्ततः पूर्वमेव तं निकरोतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That Indra, unharmed by anyone, brings down (नि चिकीषते (*ni cikīṣate*)) the wrath (मन्युं (*manyuṃ*)) of mortals. Is it only after the wrath has arrived? He says no: he desires to do so before (पुरा (*purā*)) the reproach (निदः (*nidaḥ*)). The meaning is that when anyone wishes to reproach him, he brings that person down even before that.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सः (*saḥ*)—the renowned Indra
- मर्त्यानां (*martyānāṃ*)—of mortals
- मन्युं (*manyuṃ*)—the wrath
- अदब्धः (*adabdhaḥ*)—without being harmed by anyone
- नि चिकीषते (*ni cikīṣate*)—makes it come down
- निदः (*nidaḥ*)—for reproaching him
- पुरा (*purā*)—beforehand
- चिकीषते (*cikīṣate*)—makes such persons **NOTE** humble

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The renowned Indra, without being harmed by anyone, makes the wrath of mortals come down. If anyone wishes to reproach him, he beforehand makes such persons **NOTE** humble.

### Printed p. 184

##### English translation
Unharmed he brings low the wrath of mortals; before any one can reproach him, he brings him low.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क्रत्व॒ इत्पू॒र्णमु॒दरं॑ तु॒रस्या॑स्ति विध॒तः ।  
> वृ॒त्र॒घ्नः सो॑म॒पाव्नः॑ ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> क्रत्वः । इत् । पूर्णम् । उदरम् । तुरस्य । अस्ति । विधतः ।  
> वृत्रऽघ्नः । सोमऽपाव्नः ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तुरस्य त्वरमाणस्य वृत्रघ्नः वृत्रं हतवतः सोमपाव्नः सोमपातुः उदरं क्रत्व इत् कर्मणैव पूर्णम् अस्ति भवति । कस्य कर्मणेति उच्यते । विधतः परिचरतो यजमानस्य । यतः परिचरणाभावे तस्य कुक्षिपूर्त्यभावोऽतस्तत्पूर्तये परिचरतेति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The belly of the swift Soma-drinker, the slayer of Vṛtra, is filled by the action alone (क्रत्व इत् (*kratva it*)). Whose action? It is said: of the sacrificer who serves (विधतः (*vidhataḥ*)). For without that service his belly would not be filled; therefore, for the filling of it, "serve" is to be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- तुरस्य (*turasya*)—swiftly
- वृत्रघ्नः (*vṛtraghnaḥ*)—of Indra, the destroyer of Vṛtra
- सोमपाव्नः (*somapāvnaḥ*)—and drinker of Soma
- उदरं (*udaraṃ*)—the belly
- विधतः (*vidhataḥ*)—of the sacrificer who attends him with food
- क्रत्व इत् (*kratva it*)—by the acts of offering oblations and the like
- पूर्णं (*pūrṇaṃ*)—filled
- अस्ति (*asti*)—becomes

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The belly of Indra, the swift destroyer of Vṛtra and drinker of Soma, is filled by the oblation-offering and other acts of the sacrificer who attends him.

##### English translation
The belly of the Soma-drinker, the eager slayer of Vritra, is filled by the sacrificer's offering.

### Printed p. 185

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वे वसू॑नि॒ संग॑ता॒ विश्वा॑ च सोम॒ सौभ॑गा ।  
> सु॒दात्वप॑रिह्वृता ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वे इति । वसूनि । सम्ऽगता । विश्वा । च । सोम । सौभगा ।  
> सुऽदातु । अपरिऽह्वृता ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वे त्वयि वसूनि धनान्यस्मदिष्टानि संगता संगतानि । तथा हे सोम सोमवन्निंद्र त्वयि विश्वा सर्वाणि सौभगा सौभाग्यानि संगतानि । तथा सुदातु सुदानानि अपरिह्वृता अकुटिलानि । अतस्तानि कुर्विति भावः । यद्वा । इंद्रः सोमं पीत्वा सोम इत्यभिहितः सोमश्रुतेः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, in you are gathered the riches we desire; likewise, O Soma, that is, Indra who has Soma, in you all good fortunes are gathered; likewise good gifts that are unswerving (not crooked). Therefore make them so: this is the sense. Or else: Indra, having drunk Soma, is called Soma, from the Vedic text about Soma.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सोमु (*somu*)—O Indra, possessor of Soma
- त्वे (*tve*)—in you alone
- वसूनि (*vasūni*)—all riches
- विश्वा (*viśvā*)—all
- सौभगा च (*saubhagā ca*)—and auspicious things
- अपरिहृता (*aparihṛtā*)—faultless
- सुदातु (*sudātu*)—and excellent gifts, have come together

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, possessor of Soma, in you alone all riches, all auspicious things and faultless excellent gifts have come together.

##### English translation
In you, O drinker of the Soma, are treasures stored, and all precious things and unblemished gifts.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वामिद्य॑व॒युर्मम॒ कामो॑ ग॒व्युर्हि॑रण्य॒युः ।  
> त्वाम॑श्व॒युरेष॑ते ॥ ९ ॥

### Printed p. 186

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वाम् । इत् । यवऽयुः । मम । कामः । गव्युः । हिरण्यऽयुः ।  
> त्वाम् । अश्वऽयुः । आ । ईषते ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> त्वामित् त्वामेव एषते । किम् । मम कामः । स कीदृशः । यवयुः यवेच्छुः सन्नेषते । तथा गव्युः सन्नेषते । तथा हिरण्ययुः च सन्नेषते । तथा अश्वयुः च सन्नेषते । तं काममाप्तेच्छं कुर्विति भावः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

My desire goes out to you alone (त्वामित् (*tvāmit*)). Of what kind is it? It goes out wishing for barley (यवयुः (*yavayuḥ*)); likewise it goes out desiring cows (गव्युः (*gavyuḥ*)); likewise desiring gold (हिरण्ययुः (*hiraṇyayuḥ*)); and likewise desiring horses (अश्वयुः (*aśvayuḥ*)). Fulfil that desire: this is the sense.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यवयुः (*yavayuḥ*)—that which desires barley (O Indra)
- गव्युः (*gavyuḥ*)—that which desires cows
- हिरण्ययुः (*hiraṇyayuḥ*)—that which desires gold
- अश्वयुः (*aśvayuḥ*)—and that which seeks horses
- मम (*mama*)—my
- कामः (*kāmaḥ*)—desire
- त्वां इत् (*tvāṃ it*)—you alone
- एषते (*eṣate*)—seeks

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, my desire, which seeks barley, cows, gold and horses, seeks you alone.

##### English translation
To you my desire hastens, seeking barley, cows and gold-to you it hastens seeking horses.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तवेदिं॑द्रा॒हमा॒शसा॒ हस्ते॒ दात्रं॑ च॒ना द॑दे ।  
> दि॒नस्य॑ वा मघव॒न्संभृ॑तस्य वा पू॒र्धि यव॑स्य का॒शिना॑ ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तव । इत् । इंद्र । अहम् । आऽशसा । हस्ते । दात्रम् । चन । आ । ददे ।  
> दिनस्य । वा । मघऽवन् । सम्ऽभृतस्य । वा । पूर्धि । यवस्य । काशिना ॥ १० ॥

### Printed p. 187

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र तवेत् तवैव आशसा आशंसनेन त्वमस्मदीयं क्षेत्रं यवसमृद्धं करोषीत्याशंसनेन हस्ते दात्रं चन लवनसाधनं दात्रमपि आ ददे स्वीकरोमि । किमनेन प्रयासेन । दिनस्य वा पूर्वमेव छिन्नस्य वा यवस्य संभृतस्य वा पूर्वमेव छित्त्वा निष्कृतस्य राशीकृतस्य वा यवस्य काशिना मुष्टिना पूर्धि पूरय । आशंसनं देहि च ॥ ॥ ३२ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, with this prayer of mine to you alone (आशसा (*āśasā*)), the prayer that you make our field rich in barley, I take in my hand even the sickle (दात्रं (*dātraṃ*)), the tool for reaping. Of what use is this toil? Fill (पूर्धि (*pūrdhi*)) with a handful (काशिना (*kāśinā*)) of barley that has been cut beforehand, or that has been cut and gathered and piled up beforehand; and grant the prayer.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—O wealthy
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—Indra
- तव आशसा इत् (*tava āśasā it*)—only in the hope of your help
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- हस्ते (*haste*)—in my hand
- दात्रं चन (*dātraṃ cana*)—the sickle, the tool for cutting barley
- आ ददे (*ā dade*)—I take in my hand
- दिनस्य वा (*dinasya vā*)—even if it was cut before I reap
- वा (*vā*)—or
- सुभृतस्य (*subhṛtasya*)—even if piled up beforehand, such
- यवस्य (*yavasya*)—filled with barley grain
- काशिना (*kāśinā*)—with a handful
- पूर्धि (*pūrdhi*)—fulfil my desire

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O wealthy Indra, only in the hope of your help I take in my hand the sickle, the tool for cutting barley. Even if the barley was cut before I reap, or piled up beforehand, fulfil my desire with a handful filled with such barley.

##### English translation
I take my sickle also in hand, Indra, with a prayer to you ; fill it, Maghavan, with a handful of barley already cut or piled.

## Sūkta 79 (Maṇḍala 8)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ‘ अयं कृत्नुः' इति नवर्चं दशमं सूक्तम् । अत्रेयमनुक्रमणिका - अयं कृत्नुर्नव कृत्नुर्भार्गवः सौम्यमंत्यानुष्टुप् ' इति । भार्गवः कृत्नुर्ऋषिः । नवम्यनुष्टुप् । अष्टौ गायत्र्यः । सोमो देवता । विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning अयं कृत्नुः (*ayaṃ kṛtnuḥ*) has nine Ṛks and is the tenth sūkta. The seer is Kṛtnu Bhārgava, as the Anukramaṇikā states; the ninth Ṛk is in Anuṣṭubh metre and the eight others are Gāyatrī. The deity is Soma. The use (viniyoga) is by indication (laiṅgika).

### Printed p. 188

This sūkta, beginning अयं कृत्नुः (*ayaṃ kṛtnuḥ*), is the tenth sūkta in the eighth Anuvāka. It has nine Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Bhārgava Kṛtnu; in the Anukramaṇikā it is said that he is the seer, with the deity Soma and the last Ṛk in Anuṣṭubh. Soma is the deity; the ninth Ṛk of this sūkta is in Anuṣṭubh metre, and the remaining Ṛks are in Gāyatrī metre. The use of the sūkta is by indication.

Sūkta 79

Maṇḍala 8, Anuvāka 8, Sūkta 79

Aṣṭaka 6, Adhyāya 5 **NOTE**, Varga 33, 34

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 9

Seer: Kṛtnu Bhārgava

Deity: Agni **NOTE**

Metre: Ṛks 1-8 Gāyatrī; Ṛk 9 Anuṣṭubh

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒यं कृ॒त्नुरगृ॑भीतो विश्व॒जिदु॒द्भिदित्सोमः॑ ।  
> ऋषि॒र्विप्रः॒ काव्ये॑न ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अयम् । कृत्नुः । अगृभीतः । विश्वऽजित् । उत्ऽभित् । इत् । सोमः ।  
> ऋषिः । विप्रः । काव्येन ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयं सोमः कृत्नुः कर्ता सर्वस्य अगृभीतः अन्यैरगृहीतः विश्वजित् सर्वस्य जेता उद्भित् फलस्योद्भेदकः । अथवा विश्वजिदुद्भिदौ सोमयागौ। तयोर्निष्पादकत्वात्तद्रूपः । ऋषिः ज्ञानवान् विप्रः मेधावी विप्रवत् पूज्यो विशेषेण पूरको वा । एवंमहानुभावः सोमः काव्येन स्तोत्रेण स्तुत्यो भवतीति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Soma is the maker (कृत्नुः (*kṛtnuḥ*)) of everything; he is not seized (अगृभीतः (*agṛbhītaḥ*)) by others; he is the conqueror of all (विश्वजित् (*viśvajit*)); he is the one who breaks forth with the fruit (उद्भित् (*udbhit*)). Or else: विश्वजित् (*viśvajit*) and उद्भित् (*udbhit*) are two Soma sacrifices, and because he accomplishes them he has their form. He is a seer (ऋषिः (*ṛṣiḥ*)), one who has knowledge; विप्रः (*vipraḥ*), one who is intelligent, worthy of worship like a learned priest, or one who especially fulfils. Soma of such greatness is to be praised with a hymn (काव्येन (*kāvyena*)); this is what is to be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- कृत्नुः (*kṛtnuḥ*)—the doer of all actions
- अगृभीतः (*agṛbhītaḥ*)—who is not caught by others
- विश्वजित् (*viśvajit*)—who conquers everything
- उद्भित् (*udbhit*)—the producer of fruit
- ऋषिः (*ṛṣiḥ*)—the wise one

### Printed p. 189

- विप्रः (*vipraḥ*)—and the intelligent one
- अयं सोम इत् (*ayaṃ soma it*)—this Soma
- काव्येन (*kāvyena*)—is praised by a hymn alone

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

This god Soma, the doer of all actions, who is not caught by others, who conquers everything, the producer of fruit, the wise and intelligent one, is praised by a hymn alone.

##### English translation
This all-creating Soma, obstructed by none, the conqueror of all, the producer of fruit, the seer, the wise, (is to be praised) with a hymn.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒भ्यू॑र्णोति॒ यन्न॒ग्नं भि॒षक्ति॒ विश्वं॒ यत्तु॒रम् ।  
> प्रेमं॒धः ख्य॒न्निः श्रो॒णो भू॑त् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अभि । ऊर्णोति । यत् । नग्नम् । भिषक्ति । विश्वम् । यत् । तुरम् ।  
> प्र । ईम् । अंधः । ख्यत् । निः । श्रोणः । भूत् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयं सोमः यन्नग्नम् अस्ति तत् अभ्यूर्णोति आच्छादयति । यन्नग्नमिव विफलं वर्तते तदाच्छादयति फलेन । अथवा वस्त्रं जनयन्नाच्छादयति । तथा यत् तुरम् आतुरं रुग्णं विश्वं तत् भिषक्ति भिषज्यति। यज्ञद्वारा स्वर्गसाधनेनौषधरूपेण च शरीरसिद्धिसाधनश्च । अंधः सन् अंधोऽपि प्र ख्यत् पश्यति । श्रोणः अपि पंगुरपि निः भूत् निर्भवति निर्गच्छति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Soma covers (अभ्यूर्णोति (*abhyūrṇoti*)) whatever is naked; that is, whatever remains fruitless, as if naked, he covers with fruit; or else, producing cloth, he covers it. Likewise whatever is afflicted (तुरम् (*turam*)), that is, sick, all that he heals (भिषक्ति (*bhiṣakti*)), through the sacrifice as the means of heaven and in the form of medicine as the means of the perfection of the body. The blind man sees (प्र ख्यत् (*pra khyat*)); the lame man too comes forth (निः भूत् (*niḥ bhūt*)), goes out.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—whatever thing
- नग्नं (*nagnaṃ*)—is without covering, that
- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—this god Soma
- अभ्यूर्णोति (*abhyūrṇoti*)—covers with a covering
- यत् (*yat*)—whatever
- तुरं (*turaṃ*)—is afflicted with disease
- विश्वं (*viśvaṃ*)—all that
- भिषक्ति (*bhiṣakti*)—he cures with medicine
- अंधः सन् (*aṃdhaḥ san*)—though blind
- प्र ख्यत् (*pra khyat*)—sees especially
- श्रोणः (*śroṇaḥ*)—though lame
- निः भूत् (*niḥ bhūt*)—moves about

### Printed p. 190

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Whatever thing is without covering, this god Soma covers with a covering. Whatever is afflicted with disease, all that he cures with medicine. Though blind, he sees especially. Though lame, he moves about.

##### English translation
He covers what is naked, he heals all that is sick, the blind sees, the lame walks.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वं सो॑म तनू॒कृद्भ्यो॒ द्वेषो॑भ्यो॒ऽन्यकृ॑तेभ्यः ।  
> उ॒रु यं॒तासि॒ वरू॑थम् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वम् । सोम । तनूकृत्ऽभ्यः । द्वेषःऽभ्यः । अन्यऽकृतेभ्यः ।  
> उरु । यंता । असि । वरूथम् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सोम त्वं तनूकृद्यःकृ कृशीकुर्वद्भ्यः । अथवांगानां विच्छेदकेभ्यः अन्यकृतेभ्यः द्वेषोभ्यः शत्रुकृतेभ्योऽप्रियेभ्यः । कृत्याभ्य इत्यर्थः । वरूथं वरकं रक्षणम् उरु यंतासि भवसि स्तोतॄणाम्। 'अन्यकृतानि हि रक्षांसि ' इति ब्राह्मणम् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Soma, you are for us a wide (उरु (*uru*)) protector (वरूथं (*varūthaṃ*), a choice protection) of the singers, against the hostile ones (द्वेषोभ्यः (*dveṣobhyaḥ*)), that is, the unpleasant things done by enemies, the sorceries, which make thin (तनूकृद्भ्यः (*tanūkṛdbhyaḥ*)) or, as an alternative, which cut off the limbs, and which are made by others (अन्यकृतेभ्यः (*anyakṛtebhyaḥ*)). He cites the Brāhmaṇa: अन्यकृतानि हि रक्षांसि (*anyakṛtāni hi rakṣāṃsi*) **GLOSS**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सोम (*soma*)—O god Soma
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- तनूकृद्भ्यः (*tanūkṛdbhyaḥ*)—who injure our body and make it thin
- अन्यकृतेभ्यः (*anyakṛtebhyaḥ*)—and are of demon form
- द्वेषोभ्यः (*dveṣobhyaḥ*)—from the harm of enemies
- उरु (*uru*)—abundantly
- वरूथं (*varūthaṃ*)—as the giver of protection
- यंतासि (*yaṃtāsi*)—you become

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O god Soma, you give us abundant protection against the harm of enemies who injure our body and make it thin, and who are of demon form.

### Printed p. 191

##### English translation
Soma, you offer us a wide shelter from the wasting enmities wrought by our foes.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वं चि॒त्ती तव॒ दक्षै॑र्दि॒व आ पृ॑थि॒व्या ऋ॑जीषिन् ।  
> यावी॑र॒घस्य॑ चि॒द्द्वेषः॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वम् । चित्ती । तव । दक्षैः । दिवः । आ । पृथिव्याः । ऋजीषिन् ।  
> यावीः । अघस्य । चित् । द्वेषः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ऋजीषिन् तृतीयसवनगतेनर्जीषेण तद्वन् सोम त्वं तव चित्ती चित्त्या प्रज्ञया दक्षैः बलैश्च दिव आ दिवश्च । आ इति चार्थे । पृथिव्याः आ पृथिव्याश्च सकाशात् अघस्य चित् अस्माकमाहंतुरपि द्वेषः शत्रोः कृत्यां यावीः पृथक्कुरु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Soma, you who have the Ṛjīṣa belonging to the third pressing (ऋजीषिन् (*ṛjīṣin*)): by your wisdom (चित्ती (*cittī*)) and by your powers (दक्षैः (*dakṣaiḥ*)), from heaven and from earth, put apart (यावीः (*yāvīḥ*)) separately the sorcery of the enemy, even of one who strikes us (अघस्य चित् (*aghasya cit*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋजीषिन् (*ṛjīṣin*)—O Indra, possessor of the Soma called Ṛjīṣa **NOTE**
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- तव (*tava*)—your
- चित्ती (*cittī*)—by wisdom
- दक्षैः (*dakṣaiḥ*)—and by powers
- दिव आ (*diva ā*)—those in the world of heaven
- पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*)—those on the earth
- अघस्य चित् (*aghasya cit*)—and those who wish us harm
- द्वेषः (*dveṣaḥ*)—the sinful deed of the enemies
- यावीः (*yāvīḥ*)—make it not cling to us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, possessor of the Soma called Ṛjīṣa, by your wisdom and by your powers make the sinful deed of the enemies, those in the world of heaven, those on the earth, and those who wish us harm, not cling to us.

##### English translation
O Rijishin, by your wisdom and might, drive away the enmity of our oppressor from the heaven and the earth.

### Printed p. 192

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒र्थिनो॒ यंति॒ चेदर्थं॒ गच्छा॒निद्द॒दुषो॑ रा॒तिम् ।  
> व॒वृ॒ज्युस्तृष्य॑तः॒ काम॑म् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अर्थिनः । यंति । च । इत् । अर्थम् । गच्छान् । इत् । ददुषः । रातिम् ।  
> ववृज्युः । तृष्यतः । कामम् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अर्थिनः धनानि कामयमानाः यंति चेत् यंति च अर्थं प्रति । गत्वा च ददुषः दातुः रातिं दानं गच्छानित् गच्छंति च । गतेषु मध्ये यं हे सोम त्वमनुगृह्णासि तस्य तृष्यतः भिक्षमाणस्य कामं ववृज्युः । पुनः कामानावर्जयंति । तावत्पर्यंतं पूरयंतीत्यर्थः ॥ ॥ ३३ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Seekers (अर्थिनः (*arthinaḥ*)), desiring wealth, go toward wealth (अर्थं (*arthaṃ*)), and having gone they go to the gift (रातिं (*rātiṃ*)) of the giver (ददुषः (*daduṣaḥ*)). Among those who have gone, the one whom you, O Soma, favour, the desire (कामं (*kāmaṃ*)) of that thirsty (तृष्यतः (*tṛṣyataḥ*)), begging man they fulfil (ववृज्युः (*vavṛjyuḥ*)); again they satisfy his desires. The meaning is that they fulfil it up to that extent.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अर्थिनः (*arthinaḥ*)—those who desire wealth
- अर्थं च इत् (*arthaṃ ca it*)—desiring wealth itself
- यंति (*yaṃti*)—go searching for it
- ददुषः (*daduṣaḥ*)—of the generous man
- रातिं (*rātiṃ*)—expecting the gift
- गच्छानित् (*gacchānit*)—they go (O Soma)
- तृष्यते (*tṛṣyate*)—of the petitioner who among these has obtained your favour
- कामं (*kāmaṃ*)—the desired object
- ववृज्यः (*vavṛjyaḥ*)—they fill up

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Those who desire wealth, desiring wealth itself, go searching for it. They go expecting the gift of the generous man. O Soma, among these they fulfil the desired object of the petitioner who has obtained your favour.

##### English translation
The petitioners seek for wealth, they attend the bounty of the liberal ; (by you) men pour out the desire of the thirsty.

### Printed p. 193

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वि॒दद्यत्पू॒र्व्यं न॒ष्टमुदी॑मृता॒युमी॑रयत् ।  
> प्रेमायु॑स्तारी॒दती॑र्णम् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विदत् । यत् । पूर्व्यम् । नष्टम् । उत् । ईम् । ऋतऽयुम् । ईरयत् ।  
> प्र । ईम् । आयुः । तारीत् । अतीर्णम् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यत् यदा पूर्व्यं पुराणं नष्टं स्वकीयं धनं विदत् लभते नष्टधनः ईम् एनम् ऋतायुं नष्टधनलाभार्थं यज्ञकामम् उत् ईरयत् प्रेरयति । धनं साधयतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

When one who has lost his wealth obtains (विदत् (*vidat*), gets) his own old (पूर्व्यं (*pūrvyaṃ*)) lost (नष्टं (*naṣṭaṃ*)) wealth, then Soma urges up (उत् ईरयत् (*ut īrayat*)) this one who desires sacrifice (ऋतायुं (*ṛtāyuṃ*)), for the recovery of the lost wealth. The meaning is that he brings about the wealth.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- पूर्व्यं (*pūrvyaṃ*)—old
- नष्टं (*naṣṭaṃ*)—and lost wealth
- विदत् (*vidat*)—to obtain
- ऋतायुं (*ṛtāyuṃ*)—the one who desires sacrifice
- ईं (*īṃ*)—the sacrificer (Soma)
- उदीरयत् (*udīrayat*)—urges on, then
- अतीर्णं (*atīrṇaṃ*)—devoted to sacrifice
- ईं आयुः (*īṃ āyuḥ*)—the life of this performer of the sacrifice
- प्र तारीत् (*pra tārīt*)—he lengthens

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When this Soma urges on the sacrificer who desires sacrifice, so that he may obtain his old and lost wealth, then he lengthens the life of this performer of the sacrifice, who is devoted to sacrifice.

##### English translation
(Soma) urges him on when the sacrificer obtains ( by offerings ) his old lost wealth, he lengthens out his unending life.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सु॒शेवो॑ नो मृळ॒याकु॒रदृ॑प्तक्रतुरवा॒तः ।  
> भवा॑ नः सोम॒ शं हृ॒दे ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

### Printed p. 194

> सुऽशेवः । नः । मृळयाकुः । अदृप्तऽक्रतुः । अवातः ।  
> भव । नः । सोम । शम् । हृदे ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सोम पीतस्त्वं नः अस्माकं हृदे हृदये वर्तमानः सुशेवः सुसुखः मृळयाकुः सुखयिता अदृप्तक्रतुः अप्रमत्तप्रज्ञः अवातः अगमनश्च [ तव हृदये पीताय हृदय ? ] सं (?शं) भव । अपरो नः पूरणः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Soma, having been drunk, you who dwell in our heart (हृदे (*hṛde*)) are most gracious (सुशेवः (*suśevaḥ*), very pleasant), a giver of comfort (मृळयाकुः (*mṛḷayākuḥ*)), of undimmed wisdom (अदृप्तक्रतुः (*adṛptakratuḥ*), one whose wisdom is not careless), and not moving away (अवातः (*avātaḥ*)); be auspicious (शं (*śaṃ*)) for us. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सोम (*soma*)—O god Soma
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- हृदे (*hṛde*)—in the heart
- सुशेवः (*suśevaḥ*)—who dwell happily
- मृळयाकुः (*mṛḷayākuḥ*)—who give happiness to us also
- अदृप्तक्रतुः (*adṛptakratuḥ*)—who have a wisdom free from error
- अवातः (*avātaḥ*)—and who do not go elsewhere
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- शं (*śaṃ*)—auspicious
- भव (*bhava*)—be

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O god Soma, dwelling happily in our heart, giving happiness to us also, with a wisdom free from error, and not going elsewhere, be auspicious to us.

##### English translation
Most gracious and conferring joy, void of pride in your acts, and never failing, dwell, Soma, auspiciously in our hearts.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मा नः॑ सोम॒ सं वी॑विजो॒ मा वि बी॑भिषथा राजन् ।  
> मा नो॒ हार्दि॑ त्वि॒षा व॑धीः ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मा । नः । सोम । सम् । वीविजः । मा । वि । बीभिषथाः । राजन् ।  
> मा । नः । हार्दि । त्विषा । वधीः ॥ ८ ॥

### Printed p. 195

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)
