But since it is imagined that, when she brought the Tṛtīyasavana with her beak, she bit the Soma plant a little, pressed out the juice and drank it, at the time of the Tṛtīyasavana new Soma plants are not used: after the juice has been pressed out once in the Prātaḥsavana and the Mādhyaṃdina savana, the juice that remains is pressed out well a second time from the crushed plants that are left over like husks (ṛjīṣa). Since the juice taken out in this way is not abundant, they add admixtures (āśira: milk, curds, flour) to it and make it fit for drinking.

## Sūkta 83 (Maṇḍala 8)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ‘देवानाम्' इति नवर्चं तृतीयं सूक्तं काण्वस्य कुसीदिन आर्षं गायत्रं वैश्वदेवम् । तथा चानुक्रम्यते - ' देवानां वैश्वदेवम् ' इति । दशरात्रेऽष्टमेऽहनि वैश्वदेवशस्त्र इदं सूक्तं वैश्वदेवनिविद्धानम् । सूत्रितं च -- देवानामिदव इति वैश्वदेवम् ' ( आश्व. श्रौ. ८. १०) इति ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The third sūkta, of nine Ṛks, beginning देवानाम् (*devānām*), is revealed to Kusīdin of the Kaṇva family; its metre is Gāyatrī and its deity the Viśvedevas. So the Anukramaṇikā says: देवानां वैश्वदेवम् (*devānāṃ vaiśvadevam*). On the eighth day of the Daśarātra, in the Vaiśvadeva śastra, this sūkta is used as the Vaiśvadeva nivid-dhāna. It is stated in the sūtra: देवानामिदव इति वैश्वदेवम् (*devānāmidava iti vaiśvadevam*) (Āśv. Śrau. 8. 10).

The Anuvāda: this sūkta beginning देवानां (*devānāṃ*) is the third sūkta in the ninth Anuvāka. It has nine Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is the one called Kusīdī, born in the family of Kaṇva; the Viśvedevas are the deity; the metre is Gāyatrī. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: देवानां वैश्व-देवं (*devānāṃ vaiśva-devaṃ*). On the eighth day of the sacrifice called Daśarātra, at the time of reciting the Vaiśvadeva śastra mantras, this sūkta is to be inserted and recited as the Vaiśvadeva nivid-dhāna sūkta; this is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrautasūtra देवानामिदव इति वैश्वदेवं (*devānāmidava iti vaiśvadevaṃ*) (Ā. 8-10).

### Printed p. 232

Sūkta 83

Maṇḍala 8, Anuvāka 9, Sūkta 83

Aṣṭaka 6, Adhyāya 6, Varga 3, 4

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 9

Seer: Kusīdī Kāṇvaḥ

Deity: Viśvedevāḥ

Metre: Gāyatrī

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दे॒वाना॒मिदवो॑ म॒हत्तदा वृ॑णीमहे व॒यम् ।  
> वृष्णा॑म॒स्मभ्य॑मू॒तये॑ ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> देवानाम् । इत् । अवः । महत् । तत् । आ । वृणीमहे । वयम् ।  
> वृष्णाम् । अस्मभ्यम् । ऊतये ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे देवाः देवानां स्वतेजसा सर्वतो दीप्यमानानाम् । इत् एवार्थे । युष्माकमेव महत् व्याप्तं महनीयं वा अवः पालनं यद्विद्यते तत् वृष्णां कामानां वर्षितॄणां युष्माकं स्वभूतं तत् रक्षणं यजमानाः वयम् आ वृणीमहे समंतात्संभजामहे । किमर्थम् । अस्मभ्यमूतये । पूर्वमस्मभ्यमस्मदर्थमिति साधारण्येनोक्त्वा तद्विशिनष्टि ऊतय इति । अस्माकं पालनायेति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa addresses the gods, who shine all round with their own radiance. He explains इत् (*it*) as a particle of emphasis ("only"). The great, that is, far-reaching or praiseworthy, protection that is yours alone, belonging to you who are the showerers (वृष्णां (*vṛṣṇāṃ*)) of desires, we the sacrificers solicit on all sides, that is, we seek to share in it. For what purpose? अस्मभ्यमूतये (*asmabhyamūtaye*): having first said अस्मभ्यं (*asmabhyaṃ*) in general ("for us"), he now particularises it by ऊतये (*ūtaye*): for our protection.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O gods) देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—of you, the gods
- महत् (*mahat*)—far-reaching (in respect of us)
- अवः (*avaḥ*)—whatever protection there is
- वृष्णां (*vṛṣṇāṃ*)—of you, who grant what is desired
- तत् (*tat*)—that protection
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we, the sacrificers
- अस्मभ्यं (*asmabhyaṃ*)—our
- ऊतये (*ūtaye*)—for the protection
- आ वृणीमहे (*ā vṛṇīmahe*)—pray for

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods, you who grant what is desired: we the sacrificers pray and ask of you for that protection of great nature, or protection that extends to all matters, which you wish to give us.

### Printed p. 233

##### English translation
We solicit that mighty protection of the desire-raining deities in our own behalf, for our own help.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ते नः॑ संतु॒ युजः॒ सदा॒ वरु॑णो मि॒त्रो अ॑र्य॒मा ।  
> वृ॒धास॑श्च॒ प्रचे॑तसः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ते । नः । संतु । युजः । सदा । वरुणः । मित्रः । अर्यमा ।  
> वृधासः । च । प्रऽचेतसः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ते देवाः वरुणः शत्रूणां निवारकः मित्रः सर्वेषां मित्रभूतः अर्यमा[१] सततं गच्छन् एतन्नामकास्ते त्रयो देवाः सदा सर्वदा सर्वेषु कालेषु नः अस्माकं युजः सहायाः संतु भवंतु । अग्निहोत्रादिकर्मणि इंद्रादिदेवाः सहागमनादियज्ञपरिसमापनांतेषु साहाय्यं कुर्वंत्वित्यर्थः । ततः प्रचेतसः प्रकृष्टज्ञानाः । यद्वा । चेतः स्तोत्रम् । शोभनस्तुतयः। ते देवाः वृधासः वर्धकाः च अस्माकं धनादिदानेन वर्धयितारश्च संतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa takes the gods to be three: वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*), the repeller of enemies; मित्रः (*mitraḥ*), the friend of all; and अर्यमा (*aryamā*), "ever going". May these three gods be at all times our युजः (*yujaḥ*), our helpers. The meaning is that in the Agnihotra and other rites Indra and the other gods, from their coming together until the sacrifice is completed, are to give help. Then they are प्रचेतसः (*pracetasaḥ*), of excellent knowledge. Or else (यद्वा (*yadvā*)) चेतः (*cetaḥ*) is a stotra, and the word means "those who have beautiful praises". May those gods be वृधासः (*vṛdhāsaḥ*), increasers, that is, may they make us prosper by giving wealth and the like.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ते (*te*)—those gods, that is
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—Varuṇa, the repeller of enemies
- मित्रः (*mitraḥ*)—Mitra, who is beneficial to all like a friend
- अर्यमा (*aryamā*)—Aryaman, who is always on the move; these three gods
- सदा (*sadā*)—at all times
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- युजः (*yujaḥ*)—as helpers
- संतु (*saṃtu*)—may they be
- प्रचेतसः (*pracetasaḥ*)—those gods who have excellent knowledge
- वृधासः (*vṛdhāsaḥ*)—may they be those who bring us prosperity. Or [प्रचेतसः (*pracetasaḥ*)—the excellent praises that we make; वृधासः च (*vṛdhāsaḥ ca*)—may they be the cause of our increase]

### Printed p. 234

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May those gods, that is, Varuṇa, the destroyer of enemies, Mitra, the friend of all, and Aryaman, who is always on the move, these three gods, always be helpers to us. May those gods, who are of excellent knowledge, bring us prosperity.

##### English translation
May those (deities) Varuna, Mitra, and Aryaman, be ever our allies and supremely wise helpers.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अति॑ नो विष्पि॒ता पु॒रु नौ॒भिर॒पो न प॑र्षथ ।  
> यू॒यमृ॒तस्य॑ रथ्यः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अति । नः । विष्पिता । पुरु । नौभिः । अपः । न । पर्षथ ।  
> यूयम् । ऋतस्य । रथ्यः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋतस्य सत्यस्य यज्ञस्य वा हे रथ्यः नेतारो देवाः। यद्वा । ऋतस्येति संबंधि--- त्वात्कर्ताक्षिप्यते । यज्ञस्य साधका हे रथ्यो रथवंतो देवाः विष्पिता विष्पितानि विप्राप्तानि विततानि पुरु । सुपो लुक् । पुरूणि बहूनि शत्रुबलानि कर्माणि वा नः अस्मान् अति पर्षथ पारं समाप्तिं रक्षणैर्गमयत । तत्र दृष्टांतः। नौभिरपो न । यथा नाविकोऽप उदकानि नौभिर्जनास्तीरं प्रति प्रापयति तद्वत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa addresses the gods as रथ्यः (*rathyaḥ*), leaders (charioteers) of ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*), truth or sacrifice. Or else (यद्वा (*yadvā*)), ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*) is a word of relation, and the agent is implied: O gods who accomplish the sacrifice, you who have chariots. The विष्पिता (*viṣpitā*), the widespread and many (पुरु (*puru*)) hostile forces or actions, take us across (अति पर्षथ (*ati parṣatha*)), bring us by your protections to the other shore, to completion. **NOTE**. The illustration is नौभिरपो न (*naubhirapo na*): as a boatman brings people across the waters to the shore in boats.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—of truth or of the sacrifice
- रथ्यः (*rathyaḥ*)—O gods, who are leaders. [Or ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—who are the means of the sacrifice; रथ्यः (*rathyaḥ*)—O gods, who travel in chariots]
- यूयं (*yūyaṃ*)—you
- अपः (*apaḥ*)—deep water
- नौभिः न (*naubhiḥ na*)—as one crosses with boats
- विष्पिता (*viṣpitā*)—spread all around
- पुरु (*puru*)—from the midst of the numerous enemies, or from evil deeds
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- अति पर्षथ (*ati parṣatha*)—take across

### Printed p. 235

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods, guides of the sacrifice and travellers in chariots: as one is taken across deep water with boats, so take us across the enemies, or the evil deeds, that surround us.

##### English translation
Charioteers of the sacrifice, you conduct us through the many wide-spread (forces of our enemies) as in ships across the waters.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वा॒मं नो॑ अस्त्वर्यमन्वा॒मं व॑रुण॒ शंस्य॑म् ।  
> वा॒मं ह्या॑वृणी॒महे॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वामम् । नः । अस्तु । अर्यमन् । वामम् । वरुण । शंस्यम् ।  
> वामम् । हि । आऽवृणीमहे ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अर्यमन् देव वामं वननीयं संभजनीयं धनं नः अस्माकम् अस्तु भवतु । हे वरुण शंस्यं सर्वं शंसनीयं स्तुत्य वामं धनमस्माकमस्तु । कुतः । हिशब्दो हेतौ । यस्मात्कारणाद्वयं वामं धनं युष्मान् आवृणीमहे याचामह इत्यर्थः ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa addresses Aryaman: may desirable wealth, वामं (*vāmaṃ*), that is, wealth fit to be shared, be ours. Addressing Varuṇa: may all praiseworthy (शंस्यं (*śaṃsyaṃ*)) desirable wealth be ours. Why? The word हि (*hi*) is used in the sense of a reason: because we solicit from you desirable wealth, that is, we ask it of you.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हे अर्यमन् (*he aryaman*)—O god Aryaman
- वामं (*vāmaṃ*)—wealth desired by all
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- अस्तु (*astu*)—may it be
- हे वरुण (*he varuṇa*)—O god Varuṇa
- शंस्यं (*śaṃsyaṃ*)—excellent or praiseworthy
- वामं (*vāmaṃ*)—may wealth be ours
- हि (*hi*)—because (we)
- वामं (*vāmaṃ*)—wealth
- आवृणीमहे (*āvṛṇīmahe*)—ask of you

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O god Aryaman, may wealth that all desire be ours. O Varuṇa, may excellent wealth also be ours. For we pray to you that you give us wealth.

### Printed p. 236

##### English translation
Be wealth ours, Aryaman,—wealth worthy to be praised, Varuna; it is wealth which we ask.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वा॒मस्य॒ हि प्र॑चेतस॒ ईशा॑नाशो रिशादसः ।  
> नेमा॑दित्या अ॒घस्य॒ यत् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वामस्य । हि । प्रऽचेतसः । ईशानासः । रिशादसः ।  
> न । ईम् । आदित्याः । अघस्य । यत् ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे प्रचेतसः प्रकृष्टज्ञानाः शोभनस्तुतयो वा हे रिशादसः रिशतां हिंसतां शत्रूणामसितारः क्षेप्तारो देवा यूयं वामस्य वननीयस्य धनस्य ईशानासः ईशानाः। हिरवधारणे । ईशाना एव स्वामिन एव भवथ । तस्माद्युष्मान् याचामह इत्यर्थः । ईशानाः । ईश ऐश्वर्यं । अनुदात्तेत् ।  ‘ तास्यनुदात्तेत् ' इति स्वरेणाद्युदात्तता भवति । न संबुद्धिः । ततो हे आदित्याः अदितेः पुत्रा देवाः ईम् एनं याचमानं मां तद्धनं न प्राप्नोतु यत् धनम् अघस्य पापस्य संबंधि विद्यते ॥ ॥ ३ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa addresses the gods: O Pracetasas, that is, those of excellent knowledge, or else those of fine praises; O Riśādasas, hurlers of enemies who injure. You are the lords of desirable wealth; the word हि (*hi*) is used for emphasis, so you are indeed its masters, and for that reason we ask you. Then, addressing the Ādityas, sons of Aditi: let not that wealth which is connected with sin reach me, who beg of you.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- प्रचेतसः (*pracetasaḥ*)—those of excellent knowledge, or worthy of excellent praise
- रिशादसः (*riśādasaḥ*)—destroyers of enemies; all you gods
- वामस्य (*vāmasya*)—to the wealth that all desire
- ईशानासः (*īśānāsaḥ*)—you are indeed the lords
- आदित्यासः (*ādityāsaḥ*)—O gods, sons of Aditi
- ईं (*īṃ*)—me, who beg of you
- अघस्य (*aghasya*)—connected with sin
- यत् (*yat*)—whatever wealth there is, that
- न (*na*)—let it not come to me

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods of excellent knowledge, or worthy of excellent praise, destroyers of enemies, you alone are the lords of the wealth that all desire. O gods, sons of Aditi, let no wealth connected with sin (obtained by injustice) come to me, who beg of you.

### Printed p. 237

##### English translation
Mighty in wisdom, repellers of enemies, you are the lords of wealth; be not mine the wealth, Adityas, which belongs to sin.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> व॒यमिद्वः॑ सुदानवः क्षि॒यंतो॒ यांतो॒ अध्व॒न्ना ।  
> देवा॑ वृ॒धाय॑ हूमहे ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वयम् । इत् । वः । सुऽदानवः । क्षियंतः । यांतः । अध्वन् । आ ।  
> देवाः । वृधाय । हूमहे ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सुदानवः शोभनदाना हे देवाः क्षियंतः गृहेष्वग्निहोत्रार्थं निवसंतः अध्वन् । सुपो लुक्। अध्वनि समिदाहरणार्थं यांतः गच्छंतोऽपि वयं वः इत् युष्मानेव वृधाय हविर्भिर्वर्धनाय हूमहे आह्वयामः । यद्वा । वयं गृहेषु गृहान्निर्गमनकाले मार्गेषु च वृधायास्माकं धनादिभिर्वर्धनायाह्वयामः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O bounteous gods, whether we dwell in our houses for the Agnihotra, or are going along the road to fetch fuel sticks, we invoke only you, to nourish you with oblations. Or else: we invoke you at home, at the time of leaving the house, and on the roads, for our own increase through wealth and the like.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सुदानवः (*sudānavaḥ*)—excellent givers
- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—O gods
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- क्षियंतः (*kṣiyaṃtaḥ*)—even when we stay in our houses to perform the Agnihotra and other rites
- अध्वन् (*adhvan*)—in the middle of the road, to bring fuel sticks and the like
- यांतः (*yāṃtaḥ*)—even when going
- वः इत् (*vaḥ it*)—you alone
- वृधायु (*vṛdhāyu*)—to nourish with oblations and the like, or to praise with hymns and the like, or for the increase of our wealth
- हूमहे (*hūmahe*)—we pray and call

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods, excellent givers, whether we stay at home performing the Agnihotra and other rites, or go along the road to fetch fuel sticks and the like, we pray to you and call on you alone, to praise and nourish you, or for the increase of our wealth.

### Printed p. 238

##### English translation
Bounteous deities, whether we dwell at home or go abroad on the road, we invoke you only to be nourished by our oblations.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अधि॑ न इंद्रैषां॒ विष्णो॑ सजा॒त्या॑नाम् ।  
> इ॒ता मरु॑तो॒ अश्वि॑ना ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अधि । नः । इंद्र । एषाम् । विष्णो इति । सऽजात्यानाम् ।  
> इत । मरुतः । अश्विना ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र विष्णो मरुतः हे अश्विना अश्विनौ हे इंद्रादयो देवाः सजात्यानाम् । समानायां जातौ भवाः सजात्या भ्रातृमित्रादयः । तेषाम् एषां मध्ये नः अस्मान् अधि इत यूयं स्तुत्यतयाधिगच्छत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, Viṣṇu, Maruts, Aśvins, and all you gods beginning with Indra: kinsmen (सजात्या (*sajātyā*)) are brothers, friends and the like, born in the same family. From among these, come to us, as those worthy of praise.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हे इंद्र (*he iṃdra*)—O Indra
- विष्णो (*viṣṇo*)—O Viṣṇu
- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—O Marut gods
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin gods, all of you
- सजात्यानां विषां (*sajātyānāṃ viṣāṃ*)—from among kinsmen, brothers, friends and the like, born in one family
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- अधि इत (*adhi ita*)—being prayed to, be pleased to come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, Viṣṇu, Maruts, and Aśvins, O gods beginning with Indra, being prayed to, be pleased to come to us who stand among kinsmen born in one family.

##### English translation
Come to us, Indra, Vishnu, Maruts, and Adityas, from the midst of these your brethren.

### Printed p. 239

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र भ्रा॑तृ॒त्वं सु॑दान॒वोऽध॑ द्वि॒ता स॑मा॒न्या ।  
> मा॒तुर्गर्भे॑ भरामहे ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । भ्रातृऽत्वम् । सुऽदानवः । अध । द्विता । समान्या ।  
> मातुः । गर्भे । भरामहे ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सुदानवः शोभनदाना आदित्याः अध अथ अस्मत्प्रत्यागमनानंतरं वयं समान्या सामान्येन । सुपो ड्यादेशः । पूर्वं सर्वेषां देवानां सांहत्येन ततः द्विता द्विधा द्विप्रकारेण च मातुः अदितेः गर्भे संजातं यद्युष्माकं भ्रातृत्वं विद्यते तदिदानीं वयं प्र भरामहे । प्रभरणमुच्चारणं प्रकाशनं वा । उच्चारयामः प्रकाशयामो वा । सर्वेषां देवानां द्वंद्वशो जननं तैत्तिरीयके स्पष्टमभिहितम् -- ‘ अदितिः पुत्रकामा साध्येभ्यो देवेभ्यो ब्रह्मौदनमपचत्' (तै. सं. ६. ५. ६) इत्युपक्रम्य ‘तस्यै पूषा चार्यमा चाजायेताम् ' इत्यादिना । ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O bounteous Ādityas, now, after you have come to us, we proclaim that brotherhood of yours which arose in the womb of your mother Aditi, first commonly (that is, of all the gods together) and then in two ways (द्विता (*dvitā*) means in two kinds). To bear forth here means to utter or to make known. The birth of all the gods in pairs is stated plainly in the Taittirīya text, beginning with अदितिः पुत्रकामा साध्येभ्यो देवेभ्यो ब्रह्मौदनमपचत् (*aditiḥ putrakāmā sādhyebhyo devebhyo brahmaudanamapacat*) (Tai. Saṃ. 6. 5. 6) and continuing with तस्यै पूषा चार्यमा चाजायेताम् (*tasyai pūṣā cāryamā cājāyetām*) and so on.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सुदानवः (*sudānavaḥ*)—O gods, excellent givers
- अध (*adha*)—after you have come near us, (we)
- समान्या (*samānyā*)—common
- द्विता (*dvitā*)—of two kinds
- मातुः गर्भे (*mātuḥ garbhe*)—arisen from your birth in the womb of Aditi, the mother of the gods
- भ्रातृत्वं (*bhrātṛtvaṃ*)—the brotherhood
- प्रभरामहे (*prabharāmahe*)—(knowing it) we make known

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods, excellent givers, after you have come near us, we, knowing it, proclaim the mutual bond of brotherhood that arose among you: for in the womb of Aditi, the divine mother, you were born both commonly and in two kinds.

##### English translation
Bounteous (deities), we forthwith proclaim aloud that brotherhood of yours in your mother's womb, (first) in common union, then as born in diverse manner.

### Printed p. 240

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the seer describes the gods as having been born as sons of Aditi, both by a common birth like everyone, and by a birth of two kinds (that is, in pairs); and he says that we know the brotherly relation that those two have who were thus born together in pairs. On this matter the commentator has given a passage of the Taittirīya Saṃhitā, both as an illustration and in support of the point. But in the anuvāka beginning अदितिः पुत्रकामा (*aditiḥ putrakāmā*) which the commentator names (Trai. Saṃ. 6-5-6) **NOTE** the matters quoted do not stand in one order, since many other matters are mentioned in between; and instead of quoting the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa passage that begins with these same words (अदितिः पुत्रकामा (*aditiḥ putrakāmā*)), the commentator has by a slip quoted the Saṃhitā passage. The sense set out in the Saṃhitā passage does not fit properly the intention indicated in the present Ṛk. It may be his wish that the Brāhmaṇa passage itself be quoted. Therefore there seems no need to explain that Śruti passage, that is, the Saṃhitā passage, here. And since this matter is stated plainly also in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa anuvāka beginning अदितिः पुत्रकामा (*aditiḥ putrakāmā*) (Trai. Brā. 1-1-9) **NOTE**, that is what is explained here:

अदितिः पुत्रकामा । साध्येभ्यो देवेभ्यो ब्रह्मौदनमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्प्राश्नात् । सा रेतोधत्त । तस्यै धाता चार्यमा चाजायेतां । सा द्वितीयमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्प्राश्नात् । सा रेतोधत्त । तस्यै मित्रश्च वरुणश्चाजायेतां । सा तृतीयमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्प्राश्नात् । सा रेतोधत्त । तस्या अग्ंशश्च भगश्चाजायेतां । सा चतुर्थमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्प्राश्नात् । सा रेतोधत्त । तस्या इंद्रश्च विवस्वाग्श्चाजायेतां ॥ (*aditiḥ putrakāmā | sādhyebhyo devebhyo brahmaudanamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatprāśnāt | sā retodhatta | tasyai dhātā cāryamā cājāyetāṃ | sā dvitīyamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatprāśnāt | sā retodhatta | tasyai mitraśca varuṇaścājāyetāṃ | sā tṛtīyamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatprāśnāt | sā retodhatta | tasyā agṃśaśca bhagaścājāyetāṃ | sā caturthamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatprāśnāt | sā retodhatta | tasyā iṃdraśca vivasvāgścājāyetāṃ ‖*)

(Trai. Brā. 1-1-9-1, 2, 3)

In former times Aditi, the divine mother, desiring sons for herself, cooked a special kind of oblation called brahmaudana for the gods called Sādhyas, and offered it to them. Those gods ate it and gave her the remainder. Aditi ate it and conceived. From that womb two sons were born to Aditi, Dhātā and Aryamā. Afterwards Aditi, in the same way, cooked rice a second time and offered it to them; from the remainder they gave her, she ate it, conceived again, and bore two sons, Mitra and Varuṇa. Then a third time

### Printed p. 241

she proceeded in the same way and bore two sons, Aṃśa and Bhaga. When she cooked rice a fourth time and offered it, and conceived by eating the remainder they gave, two sons were then born to Aditi, Indra and Vivasvān. This is the meaning of the passage above. Here it is said that eight sons were born to Aditi in all. This is the common birth of these gods, that is, birth from the mother's womb in the way of the world. For this reason these eight gods stand in the relation of brotherhood to one another. This the seer has set out here by the word समाना (*samānā*). Now if we inquire what द्विता (*dvitā*) means: each time Aditi conceived, two sons were born to her. In the first womb Dhātā and Aryaman, in the second womb Mitra and Varuṇa, in the third womb Aṃśa and Bhaga, in the fourth womb Indra and Vivasvant were born. The being born two by two in one womb the seer has described as द्विता (*dvitā*). Besides the common brotherhood that exists among the eight brothers, the two born in one womb have a special kind of brotherhood called द्विता (*dvitā*). It is this meaning that the seer indicates by the word द्विता (*dvitā*). This relation called द्विता (*dvitā*) applies only to the sons of Aditi born in one womb. One son born in the first womb and another son born in the second womb cannot obtain the brotherhood relation called द्विता (*dvitā*); theirs is common brotherhood. Dhātā and Aryaman, born in one womb, have the द्विता (*dvitā*) relation of brotherhood; in the same way Mitra and Varuṇa, Aṃśa and Bhaga, and Indra and Vivasvant are to be understood as having the द्विता (*dvitā*) brotherhood. The relation that holds among all these eight together is common brotherhood.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यू॒यं हि ष्ठा सु॑दानव॒ इंद्र॑ज्येष्ठा अ॒भिद्य॑वः ।  
> अधा॑ चिद्व उ॒त ब्रु॑वे ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यूयम् । हि । स्थ । सुऽदानवः । इंद्रऽज्येष्ठाः । अभिऽद्यवः ।  
> अध । चित् । वः । उत । ब्रुवे ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> पूर्वोऽर्धर्चः सिद्धः । हे सुदानवः शोभनदाना देवाः इंद्रज्येष्ठाः । इंद्रो ज्येष्ठो मुख्यो येषां ते तथोक्ताः । सर्वे देवा इंद्रनेतृका इत्यर्थः । तादृशाः अभिद्यवः अभिगतदीप्तयः यूयं हि स्थ अस्मद्यज्ञे भवथ खलु । हि प्रसिद्धौ । अध चित् अथानंतरमेव वः युष्मान् अहं ब्रुवे स्तौमि । उत अपि च पुनःपुनः स्तौमीत्यर्थः ॥ ॥ ४ ॥
> xxv. 16

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The first half-verse is already explained. O bounteous gods, you are इंद्रज्येष्ठाः (*iṃdrajyeṣṭhāḥ*), that is, those whose eldest and chief is Indra; the meaning is that all the gods have Indra as leader. Being such, and अभिद्यवः (*abhidyavaḥ*), those of approaching radiance, you indeed are present at our sacrifice; the word हि (*hi*) is used in the sense of "well known". Then, immediately after, I praise you; and the meaning of उत (*uta*) is "and moreover": I praise you again and again.

### Printed p. 242

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हे सुदानवः (*he sudānavaḥ*)—O gods, bestowers of excellent gifts
- इंद्र ज्येष्ठाः (*iṃdra jyeṣṭhāḥ*)—those who have Indra himself as leader; that is, Indra is your chief
- अभिद्यवः (*abhidyavaḥ*)—O radiant gods
- यूयं (*yūyaṃ*)—you
- स्थ हि (*stha hi*)—are you not present in our place of sacrifice
- अध चित् (*adha cit*)—immediately after, that is, after you have come to our place of sacrifice
- वः (*vaḥ*)—you
- ब्रुवे (*bruve*)—I praise
- उत (*uta*)—again and again I praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O gods, excellent givers, Indra is your chief; that is, you are under Indra's leadership. O radiant gods, are you not present in our place of sacrifice? After you have come to our sacrifice, I shall praise you again and again.

##### English translation
Bounteous (deities) with Indra as your chief, be present here in your radiance ; again and again I praise you.

## Sūkta 84 (Maṇḍala 8)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ‘प्रेष्ठं वः' इति नवर्चं चतुर्थं सूक्तं कवेः पुत्रस्योशनस आर्षं गायत्रमाग्नेयम् । तथानुक्रम्यते’ प्रेष्ठमुशना काव्य आग्नेयम्' इति । प्रातरनुवाक आग्नेये क्रतौ गायत्रे छंदस्याश्विनशस्त्रे चेदं सूक्तम् । सूत्रितं च--- युवा हि प्रेष्ठं वः' ( आश्व. श्रौ. ४. १३ ) इति । आभिप्लविकेषूक्थ्येषु मैत्रावरुणे ‘ प्रेष्ठं वः' इति तृचो वैकल्पिकः स्तोत्रियः । सूत्रितं च--’ प्रेष्ठं वो अतिथिं श्रेष्ठं यविष्ठ भारत' (आश्व. श्रौ. ७. ८) इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The fourth sūkta, of nine Ṛks, beginning प्रेष्ठं वः (*preṣṭhaṃ vaḥ*), is revealed to Uśanas, son of Kavi; its metre is Gāyatrī and its deity Agni. So the Anukramaṇikā says: प्रेष्ठमुशना काव्य आग्नेयम् (*preṣṭhamuśanā kāvya āgneyam*). This sūkta is used in the Prātaranuvāka, in the Āgneya kratu, in the Gāyatra metre, and in the Āśvina śastra. It is also laid down in the sūtra युवा हि प्रेष्ठं वः (*yuvā hi preṣṭhaṃ vaḥ*) (Āśv. Śrau. 4. 13). In the Ukthya rites of the Abhiplava days, in the Maitrāvaruṇa's part, the tṛca beginning प्रेष्ठं वः (*preṣṭhaṃ vaḥ*) is an optional stotriya; this too is laid down in the sūtra प्रेष्ठं वो अतिथिं श्रेष्ठं यविष्ठ भारत (*preṣṭhaṃ vo atithiṃ śreṣṭhaṃ yaviṣṭha bhārata*) (Āśv. Śrau. 7. 8).

The Anuvāda: this sūkta beginning प्रेष्ठं वः (*preṣṭhaṃ vaḥ*) is the fourth sūkta in the ninth Anuvāka. It has nine Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is the one called Uśanā, son of Kavi;

### Printed p. 243

Agni is the deity; the metre is Gāyatrī. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: प्रेष्ठमुशना काव्य अग्नेयं (*preṣṭhamuśanā kāvya agneyaṃ*). At the time of reciting the Prātaranuvāka mantras, this sūkta is used for the Ṛks in Gāyatrī metre connected with the Āgneya kratu and for the mantras of the Āśvina śastra, as is set out by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, युक्ष्वा हि प्रेष्ठं वः (*yukṣvā hi preṣṭhaṃ vaḥ*) (Ā. 4-13). In the Ukthya sacrifices connected with the Abhiplava, the priest called Maitrāvaruṇa is to recite the three Ṛks beginning प्रेष्ठं वः (*preṣṭhaṃ vaḥ*) as an optional stotriya tṛca, as is set out by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, प्रेष्ठं वो अतिथिं श्रेष्ठं यविष्ठ भारत (*preṣṭhaṃ vo atithiṃ śreṣṭhaṃ yaviṣṭha bhārata*) (Ā. 7-8).

Sūkta 84

Maṇḍala 8, Anuvāka 9, Sūkta 84

Aṣṭaka 6, Adhyāya 6, Varga 5, 6

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 9

Seer: Uśanā Kāvyaḥ

Deity: Agniḥ

Metre: Gāyatrī

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रेष्ठं॑ वो॒ अति॑थिं स्तु॒षे मि॒त्रमि॑व प्रि॒यम् ।  
> अ॒ग्निं रथं॒ न वेद्य॑म् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रेष्ठम् । वः । अतिथिम् । स्तुषे । मित्रम्ऽइव । प्रियम् ।  
> अग्निम् । रथम् । न । वेद्यम् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे यजमानाः प्रेष्ठं युष्माकं धनदानेन प्रियतमम् अतिथिं युप्माभिरतिथिवत्पूज्यम् । यद्वा । ‘ अत सातत्यगमने '। 'ऋतन्यंजि°' इत्यादिना अतेरिथिन् । सततं देवानां हविः प्रदातुं गच्छंतम् । मित्रमिव सखायमिव प्रियं स्तोतुः प्रीणनकरं रथं न रथमिव वेद्यम् । वेदो धनम् । धनहितं लाभहेतुम् । यथा रथी रथेन धनं लभते तद्वत्स्तोतारोऽनेन धनं लभंते । तादृशं धनलाभकारणम् अग्निं वः युष्मत्कर्मसिद्ध्यर्थं स्तुषे काव्य उशनाः स्तौमि ॥ ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O sacrificers, Agni is most dear (प्रेष्ठं (*preṣṭhaṃ*)) to you through his gift of wealth, and a guest (अतिथिं (*atithiṃ*)) to be honoured by you as a guest is. Or else: the root अत (*ata*) means constant going, and the affix ithin is added; he is the one who goes constantly to give the gods their oblation. Dear (प्रियं (*priyaṃ*)) like a friend, he is the one who pleases the praiser. He is like a chariot, वेद्यम् (*vedyam*): वेदो (*vedo*) is wealth, so he is a cause of gain, beneficial to wealth; as a charioteer gains wealth by a chariot, so the praisers gain wealth by him. Such a cause of gaining wealth, Agni, I, Uśanas Kāvya, praise for the accomplishment of your rites.

### Printed p. 244

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- प्रेष्ठं (*preṣṭhaṃ*)—him who is most dear to you (O sacrificers)
- अतिथिं (*atithiṃ*)—him who is to be honoured as a guest
- मित्रमिव (*mitramiva*)—like a friend
- प्रियं (*priyaṃ*)—beneficent
- रथं न वेद्यं (*rathaṃ na vedyaṃ*)—who brings the wealth that fills a chariot
- अग्निं (*agniṃ*)—Agni
- वः (*vaḥ*)—for the accomplishment of your rites
- स्तुषे (*stuṣe*)—I praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O sacrificers, I, called Uśanā, son of the poet, praise Agni for the accomplishment of your rites: Agni, who is dear to many, honoured like a guest, beneficent like friends, and who provides the praisers with wealth that fills a chariot.

##### English translation
I praise Agni your most beloved guest, dear as a friend, who brings wealth as a chariot;

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क॒विमि॑व॒ प्रचे॑तसं॒ यं दे॒वासो॒ अध॑ द्वि॒ता ।  
> नि मर्त्ये॑ष्वाद॒धुः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कविम्ऽइव । प्रऽचेतसम् । यम् । देवासः । अध । द्विता ।  
> नि । मर्त्येषु । आऽदधुः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अध अपि च देवासः देवा इंद्रादयः यम् अग्निं मर्त्येषु मनुष्येषु द्विता द्विधा नि आदधुः गार्हपत्याहवनीयात्मकत्वेन द्विधा निहितवंतः । तत्र दृष्टांतः। कविमिव प्रचेतसम् । प्रकृष्टज्ञानं कविं क्रांतकर्माणं पुरुषं यथा द्विधा कार्यद्वयेऽन्यो नियोजयति तद्वत् । यद्वा । दिवि पृथिव्यां च निहितवंतः । भूमौ तु हविराहरणार्थं दिवि तु हविष्प्रदानार्थमिति द्वैधं विधानं कृतवंत इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

And the gods, Indra and the others, have set Agni among mortals in two ways, as Gārhapatya and Āhavanīya. The example: as one engages a wise person (कविं (*kaviṃ*) is one of accomplished deeds) in two tasks, so the gods have done with Agni. Or else: they set him in heaven and on earth; on earth to carry the oblations, in heaven to give them; the meaning is that they made a twofold arrangement.

### Printed p. 245

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अध (*adha*)—and
- कविमिव प्रचेतसं (*kavimiva pracetasaṃ*)—as people in worldly affairs appoint a man of excellent knowledge, a wise man, to do two kinds of work, so
- देवासः (*devāsaḥ*)—the gods, Indra and the others
- यं (*yaṃ*)—which Agni
- मर्त्येषु (*martyeṣu*)—among men
- द्विता (*dvitā*)—in the two forms called Gārhapatya and Āhavanīya
- नि आदधुः (*ni ādadhuḥ*)—have established; such an Agni I praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

And as in worldly affairs people serve a man of excellent knowledge, a wise man, in order to do two good works well, so I praise that Agni whom the gods have established among men in two forms, Gārhapatya and Āhavanīya.

##### English translation
Whom the gods have set like a wise seer in a two-fold function among mortals.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्वं य॑विष्ठ दा॒शुषो॒ नॄँः पा॑हि शृणु॒धी गिरः॑ ।  
> रक्षा॑ तो॒कमु॒त त्मना॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्वम् । यविष्ठ । दाशुषः । नॄन् । पाहि । शृणुधि । गिरः ।  
> रक्ष । तोकम् । उत । त्मना ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे यविष्ठ युवतम । यद्वा । यौतेस्तृजंतस्येष्टनि रूपम् । देवानां हविषां मिश्रयितृतमाग्ने त्वं दाशुषः हविर्दत्तवतः नॄन् कर्मणां नेतॄन् यजमानान् पाहि धनादिदानेन रक्ष । नॄँः पाहीत्यत्र संहितायां नॄन्पे' (पा. सू. ८. ३. १० ) इति नकारस्य रुत्वम् । अत्रानुनासिकः' ( पा. सू. ८. ३. २ ) इति पूर्वस्यानुनासिकः । किंच गिरः त्वद्विषयाः स्तुतीः शृणुधि अवहितः संछृणु । ‘श्रु श्रवणे'। ‘श्रुशृणुपॄ' इत्यादिना हेर्ध्यादेशः । उत अपि च त्मना आत्मनैव स्वयमेव तोकम् अस्मदीयं तनयं पुत्रं रक्ष पालय । त्मनेति सर्वत्र संबध्यते । आत्मना स्वयमेव रक्ष । त्वदन्यं पालयितारं न विंदामः । त्वमेवास्मदीयं स्तोत्रं शृणु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O youngest (यविष्ठ (*yaviṣṭha*)), most youthful Agni. Or else: from the root यौ (*yau*) with the ending ṛjanta and the affix iṣṭan; the best mixer of the oblations of the gods. Protect, by giving wealth and the like, the sacrificers who have given oblations, the leaders of rites. Further, hear attentively the praises addressed to you. And protect by yourself (त्मना (*tmanā*)), of your own accord, our offspring, our son; त्मना (*tmanā*) is connected with everything: protect by yourself, since we find no protector other than you; you alone hear our praise.

### Printed p. 246

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यनिष्ठ (*yaniṣṭha*)—O Agni, most youthful
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- न्यान् (*nyān*)—the sacrificers, leaders of the rites of sacrifice
- पाहि (*pāhi*)—protect by giving wealth and the like
- गिरः (*giraḥ*)—the words of praise recited to you
- शृणुधि (*śṛṇudhi*)—hear attentively
- उत (*uta*)—and
- त्मना (*tmanā*)—yourself
- तोकं (*tokaṃ*)—our sons
- रक्ष (*rakṣa*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, most youthful, protect with wealth and the like the sacrificers who perform the rites of sacrifice. Hear attentively, with your mind, the words of praise recited to you, and yourself guard our sons.

##### English translation
Ever-youthful (Agni) protect your offerers, hear our praises, and yourself guard our offspring.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कया॑ ते अग्ने अंगिर॒ ऊर्जो॑ नपा॒दुप॑स्तुतिम् ।  
> वरा॑य देव म॒न्यवे॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कया । ते । अग्ने । अंगिरः । ऊर्जः । नपात् । उपऽस्तुतिम् ।  
> वराय । देव । मन्यवे ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अंगिरः अंगिरसां वरिष्ठ । यद्वा । अंगति सर्वत्र गच्छतीत्यंगिराः । तादृश हे ऊर्जो नपात् । नपादित्यपत्यनाम । अन्नस्य पुत्र । हविर्भिर्वर्धमानत्वात् । यद्वा । नपादिति नप्ता । हविर्लक्षणस्यान्नस्य नप्तः । ‘अग्नौ प्रास्ताहुतिः सम्यगादित्यमुपतिष्ठते । आदित्याज्जायते वृष्टिः' इति । वृष्टेरोषधय ओषधीभ्योऽग्निरित्यन्नस्य नप्ता । हे देव द्योतमान अग्ने वराय सर्वैर्वरणीयाय मन्यवे शत्रूनतिमन्यमानाय ते तुभ्यं कया कीदृश्या वाचा उपस्तुतिम् उपस्तोत्रमहं भरेयम् । त्वं महान् खल्वहमल्पः । तदर्थं स्तुतिं कुर्यामित्यृषिरग्निं प्रति वदति ॥ ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aṅgiras, chief of the Aṅgirases. Or else: he goes (अंगति (*aṃgati*)) everywhere, hence Aṅgirās. O ऊर्जो नपात् (*ūrjo napāt*): नपात् (*napāt*) is a name for offspring, so he is the son of food, since he grows by oblations. Or else नपात् (*napāt*) is a grandson: he is the grandson of the food that has the form of oblation. The oblation duly cast into Agni reaches the Sun; from the Sun comes rain; from rain, plants; from plants, Agni; thus he is the grandson of food. O shining Agni, to you who are to be chosen by all and who are scornful of enemies (मन्यवे (*manyave*)), with what sort of speech could I bring praise? You are great, I am small: so the seer says to Agni, wishing to make praise for that reason.

### Printed p. 247

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अंगिरः (*aṃgiraḥ*)—him who is famous by the name of Aṅgiras
- ऊर्जोनपातं (*ūrjonapātaṃ*)—him who is called the son of food
- देव (*deva*)—and shining
- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- वराय (*varāya*)—desired by all
- मन्यवे (*manyave*)—who disregards enemies
- ते (*te*)—to you
- कया (*kayā*)—by what sort of words
- उपस्तुतिं (*upastutiṃ*)—could I offer praise?

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, famed by the name of Aṅgiras, called the son of food, shining, desired by all, looking on enemies with disdain: by what sort of words could I compose and recite praise to you?

##### English translation
Divine Agni Angiras, son of food, with what voice (shall I utter) my praise to you, most excellent scorner of enemies?

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Aṅgiras: अंगति सर्वत्र गच्छतीत्यंगिराः (*aṃgati sarvatra gacchatītyaṃgirāḥ*). Because he pervades all regions, or because he moves everywhere, Agni has the name Aṅgiras. Or else there is a historical account of how Agni came by the name Aṅgirās.

In this sūkta and in many other Ṛks of the Ṛgveda the word Aṅgiras is used to address Agni. An explanation on this matter is needed. ये‍ऽङ्गाराः आसंस्ते‍ऽङ्गिरसो‍ऽभवन् (*ye 'ṅgārāḥ āsaṃste 'ṅgiraso 'bhavan*) (Ai. Brā. 3-34): the embers (aṅgāras) connected with Agni received the name Aṅgirasas; so it is said in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa in the story of Prajāpati's lust for his daughter. In former times Prajāpati (Brahmā) thought in his mind of his own daughter (Uṣas or Dyu) with an evil feeling (lustful desire). The gods came to know of it and thought that he must be stopped from doing this evil deed. But Prajāpati

### Printed p. 248

had no power to punish him and restrain him from this deed. Therefore all the gods together united their own terrible forms, brought forth the god called Rudra, and told him to punish this Prajāpati. Knowing this, Prajāpati took fright, assumed the form of a deer and leapt into the atmosphere. Prajāpati, in the form called the Mṛga star, can be seen even now. Then Rudra appeared in the form of the Ārdrā star and discharged an arrow at Prajāpati. Then Prajāpati's seed was spilled. The Maruts scattered that seed in various directions and burnt it up. From it (from that seed) first Āditya was born and afterwards Bhṛgu Vāruṇi. From the remaining aṅgāras (embers) the Aṅgirases came into being, so it is said.

In the Purāṇas and elsewhere the seer Aṅgirās is said to be one of the mind-born sons of Brahmā. In the Vedas this Aṅgiras is a very important seer and the original ancestor of many famous seers. This matter is found in the Vanaparva of the Mahābhārata, in the dialogue of Mārkaṇḍeya and Yudhiṣṭhira. For a reason, Agni left his duty and went off to the forest in disguise. Then the seer called Aṅgirā did Agni's work in the sacrifice, carrying the shares of oblation to the gods in place of Agni. And he prayed Agni to come back and perform his duty. Because he performed Agni's duties, this seer Aṅgirā became known as Agni's son, and the members of this seer's family as Aṅgirasas.

There are some more such stories in the Purāṇas. Besides these, in the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā itself: ते आंगिरसः सूनवस्ते अग्नेः परिजज्ञिरे (*te āṃgirasaḥ sūnavaste agneḥ parijajñire*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-62-5) it is said plainly that the seers called Aṅgirases are the sons of Agni. Why the commentator did not quote this Ṛk is not understood.

That this seer Aṅgirā belonged to a very ancient time, and that many seers of the Ṛgveda here and there call him the original ancestor of their family, is made plain by some Ṛks of the Ṛgveda.

मनुष्वदग्ने अंगिरस्वदंगिरो ययातिवत्सदने पूर्ववच्छुचे (*manuṣvadagne aṃgirasvadaṃgiro yayātivatsadane pūrvavacchuce*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-31-17) **GLOSS**

### Printed p. 249

अंगिरस्वन्महिव्रते प्रस्कण्वस्य श्रुधी हवं (*aṃgirasvanmahivrate praskaṇvasya śrudhī havaṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-45-3) **GLOSS**

दध्यङ् ह मे जनुषं पूर्वो अंगिराः प्रियमेधः कण्वो अत्रिर्मनुर्विदुस्ते मे पूर्वे मनुर्विदुः ॥ (*dadhyaṅ ha me januṣaṃ pūrvo aṃgirāḥ priyamedhaḥ kaṇvo atrirmanurviduste me pūrve manurviduḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-139-9) **GLOSS**

These Ṛks make known that the seer Aṅgirā was the forefather of many seers. Among the descendants of this seer Aṅgirā, one called Atharvan was the chief. He had three sons, Bṛhaspati, Gotama and Vṛṣaṇa. Vṛṣaṇa was the son born to this Atharvaṇa by his wife called Pathyā. His affair is indicated in many places in the Veda. Vṛṣaṇa's son was Sudhanvan Aṅgira. This Sudhanvan had three sons, Ṛbhu, Vibhvan and Vāja. These three are spoken of in the Veda as the Ṛbhus. It is said that they were pupils of Tvaṣṭṛ Prajāpati. In the family of this seer Aṅgirā there were three seers called Bṛhaspati. The Ṛbhus mentioned before and Bṛhaspati were regarded as gods, and many Ṛks in the Ṛgveda are hymns to them. Of these three Bṛhaspatis, the first, it is a matter of history, killed a Dasyu king called Paṇi and brought back the cows he had carried off, and fought with another Dasyu chief called Dāsa, who ruled on the bank of the river called Aṃśumatī, together with his ten thousand followers, and defeated him. The seer called Bharadvāja, son of another Bṛhaspati, was the priest of Divodāsa, who killed the demon called Śambara. In short, the family of the seer Aṅgirā is very great, and many famous men belonged to this family. In many places in the Ṛgveda, that is, in places where the word Agni should be used, the use of the word Aṅgira is frequent.

Ūrjo napāt: अन्नस्य पुत्र (*annasya putra*). On the question how Agni is the son of food: नपादित्यपत्यनामु । हविर्भिर्वर्धमानत्वात् (*napādityapatyanāmu | havirbhirvardhamānatvāt*); napāt means offspring or son. Since Agni is made to grow by the oblation offered into Agni, Agni can be called the son of the oblation or of food. Or else नपादिति नप्ता हविर्लक्षणस्यान्नस्य नप्तः (*napāditi naptā havirlakṣaṇasyānnasya naptaḥ*): अग्नौ प्रास्ताहुतिः सम्यगादित्यमुपतिष्ठते । आदित्याज्जायते वृष्टिः । वृष्टे-रोषधयु ओषधीभ्यो‍ऽग्निरित्यन्नस्य नप्ता ॥ अ (*agnau prāstāhutiḥ samyagādityamupatiṣṭhate | ādityājjāyate vṛṣṭiḥ | vṛṣṭe-roṣadhayu oṣadhībhyo 'gnirityannasya naptā ‖ a*). Or else the word ūrjo napāt can be given the meaning grandson of food (son's son). How Agni is the grandson of food: the oblations and the like offered into Agni go to the Sun in heaven. From the Sun comes rain (vṛṣṭi), from rain the plants and trees, and from the plants (from the trees, that is, from the arani sticks in the form of trees) Agni too is produced. Therefore Agni is said to be the napt, that is, the grandson, of the oblation or of food.

### Printed p. 250

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हे सहसो यहो (*he sahaso yaho*)—O Agni, who are born of the application of strength
- कस्य (*kasya*)—whose
- यज्ञस्य (*yajñasya*)—sacrificer's
- मनसा (*manasā*)—joined with mind, we, for you
- दाशेमु (*dāśemu*)—shall we offer the oblation?
- इदं नमः (*idaṃ namaḥ*)—this oblation or obeisance
- कत् (*kat*)—when
- वोचे उ (*voce u*)—shall I offer or speak?

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, who are born of the application of strength, according to the wish of what sacrifice or what sacrificer shall we offer oblation to you? When shall I offer you this oblation or this praise with obeisance?

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दाशे॑म॒ कस्य॒ मन॑सा य॒ज्ञस्य॑ सहसो यहो ।  
> कदु॑ वोच इ॒दं नमः॑ ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दाशेम । कस्य । मनसा । यज्ञस्य । सहसः । यहो इति ।  
> कत् । ऊं इति । वोचे । इदम् । नमः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋषिरग्निं प्रति ब्रूते । हे सहसो यहो । यहुरित्यपत्यनाम । बलेन निष्पाद्यमानत्वाद्बलस्य पुत्र हे अग्ने कस्य कीदृशस्य यज्ञस्य यज्ञवतो यजनीयदेववतो यजमानस्य मनसा युक्ताः संतो हवींषि तुभ्यं वयं दाशेम प्रयच्छेम । पूजायां बहुवचनम् । किंच तुभ्यम् इदं नमः हविर्नमस्कारं वा कत् कदा वोचे अहं वदामि । उ इति प्रश्ने । ऋषिः कदा यक्ष्यामि कदा स्तोष्यामीत्यग्निं पृच्छति । वोचे । वच्यादेशस्य लुङ्यात्मनेपद उत्तमैकवचने रूपम् ॥ ॥ ५ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The seer speaks to Agni. O son of strength (सहसो यहो (*sahaso yaho*)): यहुरित्यपत्यनाम (*yahurityapatyanāma*); since he is produced by strength, he is the son of strength. O Agni, of whose sacrifice, of what kind, that is, of what sacrificer who has a sacrifice and gods to be worshipped, joined with the mind, shall we, being so, offer oblations to you? The plural is used for honour. Further, when shall I speak to you this obeisance, or this oblation? The particle उ (*u*) is used in a question: the seer asks Agni when he shall sacrifice and when he shall praise.

### Printed p. 251

##### English translation
Son of strength, what worshipper's (offerings) shall we present to you with devoted mind, and when shall I utter to you this praise?

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अधा॒ त्वं हि न॒स्करो॒ विश्वा॑ अ॒स्मभ्यं॑ सुक्षि॒तीः ।  
> वाज॑द्रविणसो॒ गिरः॑ ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अध । त्वम् । हि । नः । करः । विश्वाः । अस्मभ्यम् । सुऽक्षितीः ।  
> वाजऽद्रविणसः । गिरः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने अध अनंतरं त्वम् । हिरवधारणे । त्वमेव अस्मभ्यं करः कुरु । देहीत्यर्थः । करोतेः । लेट्यडागमः । किमित्यपेक्षायामाह । नः अस्मदीयाः गिरः त्वद्विषयाः विश्वाः सर्वाः स्तुतीरेवं कुरु यथा सुक्षितीः । क्षियंति निवसंत्यत्रेति क्षितयो गृहाः। शोभननिवासाः । यद्वा । क्षितयो मनुष्याः । कल्याणपुत्रपौत्रादियुक्ताः । तथा वाजद्रविणसः अन्नयुक्तधनवतीः । अथवा वाजो दीप्तिः। सर्वतो दीप्तधनाश्च कुरु । त्वमस्माभिः स्तुतः सन् गृहपुत्रान्नधनादीनि देहीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, now, after this, you alone, the word हि (*hi*) being used for emphasis, do for us, that is, give. What is to be done? He says: make all our praises addressed to you such that they bring सुक्षितीः (*sukṣitīḥ*). kṣitayaḥ are houses, where people dwell; so, good dwellings. Or else kṣitayaḥ are men, possessed of good sons, grandsons and so on. And so वाजद्रविणसः (*vājadraviṇasaḥ*), possessed of food and wealth. Or else वाजो (*vājo*) is radiance: make our wealth shine everywhere. The meaning is: being praised by us, give us houses, sons, food, wealth and the like.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अथ (*atha*)—after this (O Agni)
- त्वं हि (*tvaṃ hi*)—you yourself
- अस्मभ्यं (*asmabhyaṃ*)—to us
- करः (*karaḥ*)—give wealth and the like
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- विश्वाः (*viśvāḥ*)—all
- गिरः (*giraḥ*)—words in the form of praise
- सुक्षितीः (*sukṣitīḥ*)—houses fit to dwell in, or offspring such as sons and grandsons
- वाजद्रविणसः (*vājadraviṇasaḥ*)—so that they are rich in food and wealth (make)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, may the words of praise that we recite addressed to you, through your favour give us houses fit to dwell in, or offspring such as sons and grandsons, and may those houses and the rest be rich in food, wealth and other abundance.

### Printed p. 252

##### English translation
Cause all our praises to bring to us excellent dwellings and abun- dance of wealth in food.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कस्य॑ नू॒नं परी॑णसो॒ धियो॑ जिन्वसि दंपते ।  
> गोषा॑ता॒ यस्य॑ ते॒ गिरः॑ ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कस्य । नूनम् । परीणसः । धियः । जिन्वसि । दम्ऽपते ।  
> गोऽसाता । यस्य । ते । गिरः ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे दंपते यदा गार्हपत्ये वर्तसे तदा जायापतिस्वरूपोऽसि । तस्माद्दंपतिशब्देनाग्निरभिधीयते । तथाविध हे अग्ने नूनम् इदानीं कस्य कीदृशस्य जनस्य परीणसः बहूनि धियः कर्माणि जिन्वसि प्रीणयसि । यस्य ते तव संबंधिन्यः गिरः स्तुतयः गोसाता गोसातौ गवां लाभे भवंति खलु । तस्मात्त्वं कुत्र तिष्ठसि । अस्माकमिदानीं गवेच्छा प्रवर्तते । यद्वा । हे अग्ने त्वमिदानीं कस्य कर्माणि प्रीणयसि । न कस्यापीत्यर्थः । अस्माकमेव कर्माणि प्रीणयेति भावः ॥
> अग्निमंथने ' तं मर्जयंत सुक्रतुम्' इत्येषा । सूत्रितं च -- तं मर्जयंत सुक्रतुं यज्ञेन यज्ञमयजंत देवाः' (आश्व. श्रौ. २. १६ ) इति । ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O lord of the house (दंपते (*daṃpate*)), when you are in the Gārhapatya fire you are in the form of wife and husband; therefore Agni is denoted by the word दंपति (*daṃpati*). Such an Agni, now, whose many acts (धियः (*dhiyaḥ*), rites) do you gladden? Whose praises of you bring wealth of cows? Then where do you stay? Our desire for cows is now at work. Or else: O Agni, whose rites do you now gladden? The meaning is: of no one else; gladden our rites. In the kindling of fire by friction the Ṛk तं मर्जयंत सुक्रतुम् (*taṃ marjayaṃta sukratum*) is used, and it is laid down in the sūtra तं मर्जयंत सुक्रतुं (*taṃ marjayaṃta sukratuṃ*) with यज्ञेन यज्ञमयजंत देवाः (*yajñena yajñamayajaṃta devāḥ*) (Āśv. Śrau. 2. 16). **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दंपते (*daṃpate*)—O Agni, who stand in the Gārhapatya fire-pit and are in the form of wife and husband
- नूनं (*nūnaṃ*)—now
- कस्य (*kasya*)—whose
- परीणसः (*parīṇasaḥ*)—abundant
- धियः (*dhiyaḥ*)—rites
- जिन्वसि (*jinvasi*)—gladden you?
- यस्य (*yasya*)—whose (recited by whom)
- ते गिरः (*te giraḥ*)—words of praise addressed to you
- गोसाता (*gosātā*)—can they bring the gain of cows?

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, who stand in the Gārhapatya fire, whose manifold sacrificial rites do you now delight in? To whose words of praise, recited addressed to you, are you pleased to grant cows?

### Printed p. 253

##### English translation
Whose many offerings do you gladden, Agni,—you who are the lord of the house, and whose praises bring wealth of cows ?

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तं म॑र्जयंत सु॒क्रतुं॑ पुरो॒यावा॑नमा॒जिषु॑ ।  
> स्वेषु॒ क्षये॑षु वा॒जिन॑म् ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तम् । मर्जयंत । सुऽक्रतुम् । पुरःऽयावानम् । आजिषु ।  
> स्वेषु । क्षयेषु । वाजिनम् ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सुक्रतुं शोभनप्रज्ञं सुकर्माणं वा आजिषु संग्रामेषु पुरोयावानं शत्रुहननार्थं पुरत एव गंतारं वाजिनं बलवंतं तादृशमग्निं यजमानाः स्वेषु आत्मीयेषु क्षयेषु गृहेषु मर्जयंत निर्मथितमग्निमलंकुर्वंति । परिचरंतीति यावत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sacrificers adorn in their own houses (स्वेषु क्षयेषु (*sveṣu kṣayeṣu*), गृहेषु (*gṛheṣu*)) such an Agni: सुक्रतुं (*sukratuṃ*), of good wisdom or of good deeds; पुरोयावानं (*puroyāvānaṃ*), who goes in front in battles, in order to slay the enemies; वाजिनं (*vājinaṃ*), mighty. They adorn, that is, they attend upon, the Agni who has been churned out.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सुक्रतुं (*sukratuṃ*)—the doer of good works, or the conductor of the sacrificial rites
- आजिषु (*ājiṣu*)—in battles
- पुरोयावानं (*puroyāvānaṃ*)—who rushes forward to slay enemies
- वाजिनं (*vājinaṃ*)—and mighty, such Agni, the sacrificers
- स्वेषु क्षयेषु (*sveṣu kṣayeṣu*)—in their own houses
- मर्जयंत (*marjayaṃta*)—adorn and worship

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sacrificers who perform sacrifice adorn and worship in their own houses Agni, the doer of good works, or the conductor of the sacrificial rites, who rushes forward in battles to slay enemies, and who is exceedingly mighty.

### Printed p. 254

##### English translation
They keep him bright in their houses, (Agni,) famed for glorious deeds, the mighty one who presses forward in battles.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the sacrificial rite of churning the fire, this Ṛk beginning तं मर्जयंत (*taṃ marjayaṃta*) is to be recited, as is set out by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra तं मर्जयंत सुक्रतुं यज्ञेन यज्ञमयजंत देवाः (*taṃ marjayaṃta sukratuṃ yajñena yajñamayajaṃta devāḥ*) (Ā. 2-16).

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क्षेति॒ क्षेमे॑भिः सा॒धुभि॒र्नकि॒र्यं घ्नंति॒ हंति॒ यः ।  
> अग्ने॑ सु॒वीर॑ एधते ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> क्षेति । क्षेमेभिः । साधुऽभिः । नकिः । यम् । घ्नंति । हंति । यः ।  
> अग्ने । सुऽवीरः । एधते ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अग्ने यो मनुष्यः साधुभिः साधयद्भिः क्षेमेभिः पालनैः सह क्षेति स्वगृहे निवसति । तथा यं जनं नकिः न केचन घ्रंति न हिंसंति । यः एव हंति शत्रून् स्वयमेव हंति । स मनुष्यस्तव स्तोता खलु । अन्यथा तस्यैतावन्न घटते । ततः स स्तोता सुवीरः शोभनपुत्रादियुक्तः सन् एधते आत्मीयगृहेषु धनादिभिर्वर्धते ॥ ॥ ६ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Agni, the man who dwells in his own house together with good, efficient protections; whom none harms or injures; and who himself slays his enemies, he is surely your worshipper. Otherwise this would not befit him. Therefore that worshipper, endowed with excellent sons and the like, prospers: he grows in his own house through wealth and the rest.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्ने (*agne*)—O Agni
- यः (*yaḥ*)—the man who praises you, that man
- साधुभिः (*sādhubhiḥ*)—excellent
- क्षेमेभि (*kṣemebhi*)—endowed with protections
- क्षेति (*kṣeti*)—dwells in his own house
- यं (*yaṃ*)—that man
- नकिः घ्नंति (*nakiḥ ghnaṃti*)—none harms
- यः (*yaḥ*)—that man

### Printed p. 255

- हंति (*haṃti*)—becomes able to slay his enemies
- सुवीरः (*suvīraḥ*)—endowed with heroic sons and the like
- एधते (*edhate*)—attains prosperity in his house through wealth and the rest

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Agni, the man who praises you dwells in his own house without any hindrance, endowed with excellent protections. None can harm him. He becomes able to slay his enemies, and, endowed with heroic sons and the like, he lives happily in his own house in an abundance of wealth.

##### English translation
He who dwells at home with all-efficient protections, whom none can harm, but who himself harms (his enemies),—he Agni, (your worshipper) waxes strong with heroic offspring.

## Sūkta 85 (Maṇḍala 8)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ‘आ मे हवम्' इति नवर्चं पंचमं सूक्तम् । कृष्णो नामांगिरस ऋषिः । गायत्री छंदः । एतदादीनि त्रीणि सूक्तान्यश्विदेवत्यानि । तथा चानुक्रांतम्- ' आ मे कृष्ण आश्विनं हि ' इति । प्रातरनुवाक आश्विने क्रतौ गायत्रे छंदस्याश्विनशस्त्रे चेदं सूक्तम् । सूत्रितं च--' उदीराथामा में हवम्' ( आश्व. श्रौ. ४. १५) इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning आ मे हवम् (*ā me havam*) has nine Ṛks and is the fifth sūkta. Its seer is Kṛṣṇa, of the Āṅgirasa family; its metre is Gāyatrī. This sūkta and the two that follow have the Aśvins as their deity. So the Anukramaṇikā says: आ मे कृष्ण आश्विनं हि (*ā me kṛṣṇa āśvinaṃ hi*). This sūkta is used in the Prātaranuvāka, in the Āśvina kratu, in the Gāyatra metre, and in the Āśvina śastra. It is laid down in the sūtra उदीराथामा में हवम् (*udīrāthāmā meṃ havam*) (Āśv. Śrau. 4. 15).

The Anuvāda: this sūkta beginning आ मो हवं (*ā mo havaṃ*) is the fifth sūkta in the ninth Anuvāka. It has nine Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is the one called Āṅgirasaḥ Kṛṣṇaḥ; the metre is Gāyatrī. The three sūktas from this one onward have the Aśvins as their deity. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: आ मो कृष्ण आश्विनं हि (*ā mo kṛṣṇa āśvinaṃ hi*). At the time of reciting the Prātaranuvāka mantras, this sūkta is used for the Gāyatrī-metre mantras connected with the Āśvina kratu and for the Āśvina śastra mantras, as is set out by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra उदीरथामा मो हवं (*udīrathāmā mo havaṃ*) (Ā. 4-15).

### Printed p. 256

Sūkta 85

Maṇḍala 8, Anuvāka 9, Sūkta 85

Aṣṭaka 6, Adhyāya 6, Varga 7, 8

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 9

Seer: Kṛṣṇaḥ

Deity: Aśvinau

Metre: Gāyatri

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ मे॒ हवं॑ नास॒त्याश्वि॑ना॒ गच्छ॑तं यु॒वम् ।  
> मध्वः॒ सोम॑स्य पी॒तये॑ ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । मे । हवम् । नासत्या । अश्विना । गच्छतम् । युवम् ।  
> मध्वः । सोमस्य । पीतये ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे नासत्या । असत्यमनयोर्नास्तीति नासत्यौ । तादृशौ हे अश्विना अश्विनौ युवं युवां मे मदीयं हवम् आह्वानं श्रुत्वा । यद्वा । हवम् । हूयतेऽत्रेति हवो यज्ञः । केवलस्याधिकरणेऽप् छांदसः । मदीयं यज्ञं प्रति मध्वः मदकरस्य सोमस्य पीतये पानाय तदर्थम् आ गच्छतम् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Nāsatyas, that is, those in whom there is no untruth; O Aśvins, you two, having heard my call (my invocation), or else, having come to my sacrifice (the word हवम् (*havam*) being taken as that in which one is called, the sacrifice), come for the drinking of the intoxicating Soma, for the purpose of drinking it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हे नासत्या (*he nāsatyā*)—you who speak no untruth
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- युवं (*yuvaṃ*)—you
- मे (*me*)—my
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—having heard the invocation
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—intoxicating
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—of the Soma juice
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—for the drinking
- आ गच्छतं (*ā gacchataṃ*)—graciously come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, whose nature is truth, hear my invocation and graciously come here to drink the gladdening Soma.

### Printed p. 257

##### English translation
Nasatyas, Aswins, come to my invocation, that you may drink the exhilarating Soma.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मं मे॒ स्तोम॑मश्विने॒मं मे॑ शृणुतं॒ हव॑म् ।  
> मध्वः॒ सोम॑स्य पी॒तये॑ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमम् । मे । स्तोमम् । अश्विना । इमम् । मे । शृणुतम् । हवम् ।  
> मध्वः । सोमस्य । पीतये ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विना अश्विनौ मे मदीयम् इमं स्तोमं स्तोत्रं किंच मे मदीयम् इमम् इदमागमनविषयं हवम् आह्वानं च शृणुतम् ॥ ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, hear this hymn of mine, and also hear this call of mine, this invocation concerning your coming.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- मे (*me*)—my
- इमं स्तोमं (*imaṃ stomaṃ*)—this hymn, and
- मे (*me*)—my
- इमं हवं (*imaṃ havaṃ*)—this invocation
- शृणुतं (*śṛṇutaṃ*)—hear
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—intoxicating
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—of the Soma juice
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—come for the drinking

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, hear the hymn that I make in your honour and my invocation that you should come to the sacrificial ground. Graciously come here to drink the intoxicating Soma.

##### English translation
Ashwlns, hear this my hymn, this my invocation, that you may drink the exhilarating Soma.

### Printed p. 258

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒यं वां॒ कृष्णो॑ अश्विना॒ हव॑ते वाजिनीवसू ।  
> मध्वः॒ सोम॑स्य पी॒तये॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अयम् । वाम् । कृष्णः । अश्विना । हवते । वाजिनीवसू इति वाजिनीऽवसू ।  
> मध्वः । सोमस्य । पीतये ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वाजिनीवसू अन्नयुक्तधनौ । अत्रेनिरनुवादार्थः । यद्वा । वाजो वजनं क्रिया। तद्वती वाजिनी । तद्युक्तधनवंतौ हे अश्विना अश्विनौ अयं कृष्णः नाम मंत्रद्रष्टा ऋषिः वां युवां हवते स्तुतिभिराह्वयति । किमर्थम् । मध्वः सोमस्य पीतये इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O वाजिनीवसू (*vājinīvasū*), you who have wealth together with food; **NOTE** or else: vāja means going, that is, action; the one who possesses it is वाजिनी (*vājinī*); so, O Aśvins, you who possess wealth joined with it, this seer called Kṛṣṇa, the seer of the mantra, calls you with his praises. For what purpose? For the drinking of the intoxicating Soma.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वाजिनीवसू (*vājinīvasū*)—you who have food, or who have action itself as your wealth
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- अयं कृष्णः (*ayaṃ kṛṣṇaḥ*)—this seer by the name of Kṛṣṇa (I)
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—intoxicating
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—of the Soma juice
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—for the drinking
- हवते (*havate*)—prays and calls

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, rich in food, this seer by the name of Kṛṣṇa, the seer of this sūkta, prays to you and calls you to drink the intoxicating Soma.

##### English translation
Krishna invoke you, Aswins rich in sacrifices, that you may drink the exhilarating Soma.

### Printed p. 259

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> शृ॒णु॒तं ज॑रि॒तुर्हवं॒ कृष्ण॑स्य स्तुव॒तो न॑रा ।  
> मध्वः॒ सोम॑स्य पी॒तये॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शृणुतम् । जरितुः । हवम् । कृष्णस्य । स्तुवतः । नरा ।  
> मध्वः । सोमस्य । पीतये ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे नरा नरौ सर्वस्य नेतारावश्विनौ जरितुः । तच्छीलार्थे तृन् । व्यत्ययेनांतोदात्तत्वम् । जरितुः स्तवनशीलस्य संप्रति स्तुवतः स्तोत्रं कुर्वतः कृष्णस्य एतन्नामकस्यर्षेः संबंधि हवं युष्मद्विषयमाह्वानं शृणुतम् । यद्वा । जरितुरन्यदेवानां स्तोतुः स्तुवत इदानीं युवयोः स्तोत्रकारिणस्तस्य हवं शृणुतम् । शिष्टं गतम् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O leaders, that is, O Aśvins, who lead everything: hear the call, the invocation addressed to you, of Kṛṣṇa, one of that name among the seers, who is a praiser by nature, जरितुः (*jarituḥ*), who is now praising, that is, making a hymn. Or else: hear the call of the one who is now your praiser, although he is a praiser of other gods. The rest has been explained already.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हे नरा (*he narā*)—O Aśvin deities, leaders of rites or guides of all
- स्तुवतः (*stuvataḥ*)—who is making a hymn
- जरितुः कृष्णस्य (*jarituḥ kṛṣṇasya*)—of the praiser by the name of Kṛṣṇa
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—the invocation
- शृणुतं (*śṛṇutaṃ*)—hear
- जरितुः (*jarituḥ*)—or, though a praiser of other gods
- स्तुवतः (*stuvataḥ*)—who is now praising you
- कृष्णस्य (*kṛṣṇasya*)—of the seer of the mantra by the name of Kṛṣṇa
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—the invocation
- शृणुतं (*śṛṇutaṃ*)—hear
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—intoxicating
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—of the Soma juice
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—graciously come for the drinking

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, guides of all, hear the invocation of the seer by the name of Kṛṣṇa, who is a praiser and is praising you, and graciously come to drink the intoxicating Soma.

##### English translation
Leaders (of all), hear the invocation of Krishna, the hymner, who praises you,—that you may drink the exhilarating Soma.

### Printed p. 260

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> छ॒र्दिर्यं॑त॒मदा॑भ्यं॒ विप्रा॑य स्तुव॒ते न॑रा ।  
> मध्वः॒ सोम॑स्य पी॒तये॑ ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> छर्दिः । यंतम् । अदाभ्यम् । विप्राय । स्तुवते । नरा ।  
> मध्वः । सोमस्य । पीतये ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे नरा नेतारावश्विनौ विप्राय मेधाविने अत एव स्तुवते स्तोत्रं कुर्वते कृष्णायर्षये अदाभ्यम् । ‘दभेश्च' इति ण्यत्प्रत्ययः । परैरहिंस्यं छर्दिः गृहं यंतं प्रयच्छतम् । किमर्थम् । सोमपानाय । स्तोत्रे गृहे दीयमाने सति तदा स सोमं युवाभ्यां प्रयच्छति ॥ ॥ ७ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O leaders, O Aśvins: to the sage Kṛṣṇa, who is wise and therefore praises you, grant a dwelling (छर्दिः (*chardiḥ*), a house) that cannot be harmed by others (अदाभ्यम् (*adābhyam*)). For what purpose? For the drinking of Soma. When a house is given to the praiser, he offers the Soma to you both.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हे नरा (*he narā*)—O Aśvin deities, leaders
- विप्राय (*viprāya*)—to the sage Kṛṣṇa, who is wise
- स्तुवते (*stuvate*)—and who is praising you
- अदाभ्यं (*adābhyaṃ*)—free from hindrance
- छर्दिः (*chardiḥ*)—a house
- यंतं (*yaṃtaṃ*)—give
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—gladdening
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—of the Soma
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—for the drinking, graciously come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, leaders, graciously come to drink the gladdening Soma, and, satisfied with the drinking of the Soma, give to the wise sage called Kṛṣṇa, who praises you, a house free from every hindrance.

##### English translation
Leaders, give to the sage who praises you an unassailable dwelling, that you may drink the exhilarating Soma.

### Printed p. 261

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> गच्छ॑तं दा॒शुषो॑ गृ॒हमि॒त्था स्तु॑व॒तो अ॑श्विना ।  
> मध्वः॒ सोम॑स्य पी॒तये॑ ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> गच्छतम् । दाशुषः । गृहम् । इत्था । स्तुवतः । अश्विना ।  
> मध्वः । सोमस्य । पीतये ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विना अश्विनौ इत्था इत्थमनेन प्रकारेण स्तुवतः युवयोः स्तोत्रं कुर्वतः दाशुषः हवींषि दत्तवतो यजमानस्य गृहं प्रति गच्छतं युवामागच्छतम् । किमर्थम् । मध्वः सोमस्य पीतये इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, to the house of the sacrificer who thus, in this manner, praises you and has given oblations, come, both of you. For what purpose? For the drinking of the intoxicating Soma.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- इत्था (*itthā*)—in this manner
- स्तुवतः (*stuvataḥ*)—who praises you
- दाशुषः (*dāśuṣaḥ*)—and offers oblations, of the sacrificer
- गृहं (*gṛhaṃ*)—to the house
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—gladdening
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—of the Soma
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—for the drinking
- गच्छतं (*gacchataṃ*)—graciously come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, graciously come, to drink the intoxicating Soma juice, to the house of the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice, praising you in this manner and offering oblations.

##### English translation
Ashwins, come to the house of the offerer who thus praises you, that you may drink the exhilarating Soma.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> युं॒जाथां॒ रास॑भं॒ रथे॑ वी॒ड्वं॑गे वृषण्वसू ।  
> मध्वः॒ सोम॑स्य पी॒तये॑ ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 262

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> युंजाथाम् । रासभम् । रथे । वीळुऽअंगे । वृषण्वसू इति वृषण्ऽवसू ।  
> मध्वः । सोमस्य । पीतये ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वृषण्वसू वर्षणशीलधनवंतावश्विनौ युवां वीड्वंगे। वीळुर्दृढः । दृढांगोपेते स्वरथे रासभं शब्दायमानमेतन्नामकमश्वं युंजाथां संयोजयतम् । किमर्थम् । मध्वः सोमस्य पीतये इति । रासभावश्विनोः' इति रासभावेवाश्विनो रथस्य वाहनौ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, you who have wealth that showers (वृषण्वसू (*vṛṣaṇvasū*)), yoke to your chariot, which has firm parts (vīḷu meaning firm), the ass, so called because it makes a sound; yoke it. For what purpose? For the drinking of the intoxicating Soma. And he adds, with the words रासभावश्विनोः (*rāsabhāvaśvinoḥ*), that the two asses are indeed the draught animals of the Aśvins' chariot.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृषण्वसू (*vṛṣaṇvasū*)—O Aśvin deities, who pour out the desired objects
- वीळ्वंगे (*vīḷvaṃge*)—in your chariot, which has firm and various parts
- रासभं (*rāsabhaṃ*)—the ass, your draught animal
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—delicious
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—Soma
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—in order to drink
- युंजाथां (*yuṃjāthāṃ*)—join and yoke

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, givers of what is desired, in order to come here and drink the delicious Soma juice, yoke to your chariot, which has firm parts, your draught animal, the ass.

##### English translation
You who possess showering wealth, yoke the ass to your firmly-built chariot, that you may drink the exhilarating soma.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> त्रि॒वं॒धु॒रेण॑ त्रि॒वृता॒ रथे॒ना या॑तमश्विना ।  
> मध्वः॒ सोम॑स्य पी॒तये॑ ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> त्रिऽवंधुरेण । त्रिऽवृता । रथेन । आ । यातम् । अश्विना ।  
> मध्वः । सोमस्य । पीतये ॥ ८ ॥

### Printed p. 263

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विना अश्विनौ त्रिवंधुरेण त्रिफलकासंघटितेन त्रिवृता त्रिकोणेन । यद्वा । त्रिवृता। त्रीणि कवचादिभिरावरणानि यस्य स तथोक्तः । तेन रथेन अस्मद्यज्ञं प्रति आ यातं युवामागच्छतं च । शिष्टं गतम् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, with your chariot that is joined with three boards (त्रिवंधुरेण (*trivaṃdhureṇa*)) and is triangular (त्रिवृता (*trivṛtā*)); or else, त्रिवृता (*trivṛtā*) means that which has three coverings, by armour and the like; come, both of you, with that chariot to our sacrifice. The rest has been explained already.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- त्रिवंधुरेण (*trivaṃdhureṇa*)—which has three boards joined together
- त्रिवृता (*trivṛtā*)—which is triangular in form, or covered with three kinds of protective armour and the like
- रथेन (*rathena*)—with the chariot
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—gladdening
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—Soma
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—in order to drink
- आ यातं (*ā yātaṃ*)—come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, seated in your chariot, which has a yoke-pole made by joining three boards and is triangular in form, or is furnished with three kinds of protective coverings, graciously come here to drink the gladdening Soma. **NOTE**

##### English translation
Aswins, come hither with your three-seated triangular car, that you may drink the exhilarating soma.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नू मे॒ गिरो॑ नास॒त्याश्वि॑ना॒ प्राव॑तं यु॒वम् ।  
> मध्वः॒ सोम॑स्य पी॒तये॑ ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नु । मे । गिरः । नासत्या । अश्विना । प्र । अवतम् । युवम् ।  
> मध्वः । सोमस्य । पीतये ॥ ९ ॥

### Printed p. 264

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे नासत्या असत्यरहितौ हे अश्विना अश्विनौ मे मदीयाः गिरः स्तुतिलक्षणा वाचः प्रति युवं युवां नु क्षिप्रं प्रावतं प्रकर्षेणागच्छतम् । यद्वा । मे गिरः प्रावतं आत्मीयतया प्ररक्षतम् । किमर्थम् । मध्वः मदकरस्य सोमस्य पीतये पानाय तदर्थम् । सर्वे देवाः स्तुतिभिराहूताः संतो यज्ञं प्रत्यागच्छंतीति गच्छतं रक्षतं चेति युक्तं भवति ॥ ॥ ८ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Nāsatyas, free from untruth, O Aśvins, come quickly (नु (*nu*)) forward to my words of praise (गिरः (*giraḥ*)). Or else: protect my words of praise as your own. For what purpose? For the drinking of the intoxicating Soma. Since all the gods, when they are called with praises, come to the sacrifice, both "come" and "protect" are fitting.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नासत्या (*nāsatyā*)—you who utter no untrue words
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- मे (*me*)—my
- गिरः (*giraḥ*)—having regard to my words in the form of praise
- युवं (*yuvaṃ*)—you
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—gladdening
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—Soma
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—in order to drink
- नु (*nu*)—quickly
- प्रावतं (*prāvataṃ*)—come
- or मे (*me*)—my
- गिरः (*giraḥ*)—words in the form of praise
- प्रावतं (*prāvataṃ*)—may they protect me

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, who utter no untrue words, having regard to my words in the form of praise, come quickly to drink the gladdening Soma. May my words in the form of praise protect me.

##### English translation
Nasatyas, Aswins, hasten quickly to my praise, that you may drink the exhilarating soma-

## Sūkta 86 (Maṇḍala 8)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ‘उभा हि दस्रा' इति पंचर्चं षष्ठं सूक्तम् । विश्वको नाम कृष्णस्य पुत्रः कृष्ण एव वर्षिः । जगती छंदः । अश्विनौ देवता । तथानुक्रम्यते-- ’उभा हि पंच विश्वको वा कार्ष्णिर्जागतम्' इति । विनियोगो लिंगादवगंतव्यः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sixth sūkta, of five Ṛks, beginning उभा हि दस्रा (*ubhā hi dasrā*). Its seer is Viśvaka, son of Kṛṣṇa, or Kṛṣṇa himself; its metre is Jagatī; its deity the Aśvins. So the Anukramaṇikā says: उभा हि पंच विश्वको वा कार्ष्णिर्जागतम् (*ubhā hi paṃca viśvako vā kārṣṇirjāgatam*). The ritual use is to be learned from the indication (liṅga) of the text itself. **NOTE**

The Anuvāda: this sūkta beginning उभा हि दस्रा (*ubhā hi dasrā*) is the sixth sūkta in the ninth Anuvāka. It has five Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is the one called Viśvaka, son of the seer Kṛṣṇa,

### Printed p. 265

or else Kṛṣṇa himself is the seer; the Aśvins are the deity; the metre is Jagatī. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: उभा हि पंच विश्वको वा कार्ष्णिर्जागतं (*ubhā hi paṃca viśvako vā kārṣṇirjāgataṃ*). The use is by the indication of the text.

Sūkta 86

Maṇḍala 8, Anuvāka 9, Sūkta 86

Aṣṭaka 6, Adhyāya 6, Varga 9

Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5

Seer: Kṛṣṇo Viśvako vā Kārṣṇiḥ

Deity: Aśvinau

Metre: Jagatī

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒भा हि द॒स्रा भि॒षजा॑ मयो॒भुवो॒भा दक्ष॑स्य॒ वच॑सो बभू॒वथुः॑ ।  
> ता वां॒ विश्व॑को हवते तनूकृ॒थे मा नो॒ वि यौ॑ष्टं स॒ख्या मु॒मोच॑तम् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उभा । हि । दस्रा । भिषजा । मयःऽभुवा । उभा । दक्षस्य । वचसः । बभूवथुः ।  
> ता । वाम् । विश्वकः । हवते । तनूऽकृथे । मा । नः । वि । यौष्टम् । सख्या । मुमोचतम् ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विनौ दस्रा दर्शनीयौ। यद्वा । ‘दसु उपक्षये'। सर्वेषां शत्रूणामुपक्षपयितारौ । भिषजा देवानां वैद्यौ । यद्वा भीतीनां त्रासयितारौ। यदा नरा अश्विनौ स्तुवंति तदा तौ तेषां भीतिमपनयत इत्यर्थः । पृषोदरादित्वाद्रूपसिद्धिः । तादृशौ अत एव मयोभुवा मयसः सुखस्य भावयितारौ उभा परस्परं द्वित्वसंख्यापूरकौ उभा उभौ द्वौ युवां दक्षस्य एतन्नामस्य प्रजापतेः वचसः स्तुतेः संबंधिनौ बभूवथुः । हि प्रसिद्धौ । पुरा युवां दक्षेणास्ताविषाथां खलु। ता तादृशौ प्रशस्तौ वां युवां विश्वकः एतन्नामक ऋषिः तनूकृथे। तनोति कुलमिति तनूः पुत्रः । तस्य विष्णाप्वो निमित्तं हवते स्तुतिभिराह्वयति । तस्मात् नः अस्माकं सख्या सख्यानि यष्ट्टयष्टव्यतया जातानि सखित्वानि मा वि यौष्टं मा पृथकुरुतम् । यौतेर्लुङि सिचि रूपम्। किंच युवां मुमोचतम् अस्मानागंतुं रथे स्थित्वाश्वप्रग्रहान्मुंचतम् । मुंचतेर्लोटि ‘बहुलं छंदसि' इति शपः श्लुः । अडागमः । अत्र विष्णापूनामानं पुत्रमुद्दिश्य तस्य पिता विश्वको युवामाह्वयतीत्यात्मानमाह । यद्वा । विश्वकस्य पिता कृष्णो नामर्षिर्मम पुत्रो विश्वकः पुत्रार्थं युवामधिह्वयतीति वदति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, you are दस्रा (*dasrā*), that is, beautiful to behold; or else, from the root दसु (*dasu*) in the sense of destruction, you are the destroyers of all enemies. You are भिषजा (*bhiṣajā*), the physicians of the gods; or else you are the drivers away of fears: when men praise the Aśvins, they remove their fear. Being such, you are मयोभुवा (*mayobhuvā*), the producers of happiness. Both of you (उभा (*ubhā*)) have become the objects of the praise of Dakṣa, the Prajāpati of that name; this is well known (हि (*hi*)): formerly Dakṣa praised you. Such praiseworthy ones as you, Viśvaka, the seer of that name, calls with praises for the sake of his son, called तनूकृथे (*tanūkṛthe*), that is, one who spreads the family, a son, namely Viṣṇāpū (so the block reads, and later Viṣṇāpva). **NOTE** Therefore do not separate our friendships (सख्या (*sakhyā*)), which have come into being through our being worshippers; **NOTE** and further, release, that is, standing on the chariot, loosen the reins of the horses, in order to come to us. Here the father Viśvaka, speaking of himself, says that he calls you on behalf of his son named Viṣṇāpū. Or else: Kṛṣṇa, the seer who is the father of Viśvaka, says that his son Viśvaka calls you for the sake of a son.

### Printed p. 266

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दस्रा (*dasrā*)—those whose form is attractive
- भिषजा (*bhiṣajā*)—the physicians of the gods
- मुयोभुवा (*muyobhuvā*)—those who produce happiness
- उभा (*ubhā*)—you, O Aśvin deities, who are always together, both of you
- दक्षस्य (*dakṣasya*)—of Dakṣa Prajāpati
- वचसः (*vacasaḥ*)—the objects of the praise
- बभूवथुः (*babhūvathuḥ*)—you have become
- हि (*hi*)—this matter is well known
- ता (*tā*)—you who are thus well known
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you
- विश्वकः (*viśvakaḥ*)—the seer called Viśvaka
- तनूकृथे (*tanūkṛthe*)—for the sake of his son
- हवते (*havate*)—invokes with praises; therefore
- नः (*naḥ*)—in our case
- सख्या (*sakhyā*)—the friendship you have
- मा वि यौष्टं (*mā vi yauṣṭaṃ*)—do not cause it to depart, and
- मुमोचतं (*mumocataṃ*)—you who have come near us, unyoke the horses of your chariot

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, whose form is attractive, the physicians of the gods, the givers of happiness to all, who, though two, are always together, you have been the objects of the praise of Dakṣa Prajāpati. This matter is well known. The seer called Viśvaka (the seer of this sūkta) invokes you, such as you are, with praises for the sake of his son. Do not let the friendship you have for us fall away; unyoke here the horses of your chariot, you who have come near us.

##### English translation
Dasras, physicians, sources of happiness, you both were (the objects) of Daksha's praise; Vishwaka now invokes you for the sake of his son ; sever not our friendships, but fling loose (your reins and gallop hither).

### Printed p. 267

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this Ṛk the seer Viśvaka, the seer of the sūkta, speaks of himself, saying that he invokes the Aśvin deities on behalf of his son called Viṣṇāpu. Or else it may be said that the seer called Kṛṣṇa, the father of the seer Viśvaka, says that his son Viśvaka invokes the Aśvin deities on account of his own son called Viṣṇāpu.

दस्रा (*dasrā*)—दर्शनीयौ (*darśanīyau*). Those whose form is attractive. Or else दसु उपक्षये । सर्वेषां शत्रूणामुपक्षपयितारौ । (*dasu upakṣaye | sarveṣāṃ śatrūṇāmupakṣapayitārau |*). If it is supposed that the word दस्रा (*dasrā*) is formed from the root दसु (*dasu*), "to destroy", the meaning is also: those who destroy the enemies.

भिषजा (*bhiṣajā*)—Two kinds of meaning may be given for this word also. As in the scriptural sentence अश्विनौ वै देवानां भिषजौ (*aśvinau vai devānāṃ bhiṣajau*), these Aśvin deities are well known as the physicians of the gods. Or else भीतीनां त्रासयितारौ । यदा नरा अश्विनौ स्तुवंति तदा तौ तेषां भीतिमुपनयत इत्यर्थः । (*bhītīnāṃ trāsayitārau | yadā narā aśvinau stuvaṃti tadā tau teṣāṃ bhītimupanayata ityarthaḥ |*): those who remove the fear that comes upon people. That is, when people pray to the Aśvin deities, these deities remove the fear of those people. Therefore the meaning "those who remove fear" may also be given.

तनूकृथे (*tanūkṛthe*)—तनोति कुलमिति तनूः पुत्रः तस्य निमित्तं (*tanoti kulamiti tanūḥ putraḥ tasya nimittaṃ*). Because he extends the family, that is, because he causes the lineage to grow through offspring, a son is called तनूः (*tanūḥ*); the sense of the word is "for the sake of that son".

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> क॒था नू॒नं वां॒ विम॑ना॒ उप॑ स्तवद्यु॒वं धियं॑ ददथु॒र्वस्य॑इष्टये ।  
> ता वां॒ विश्व॑को हवते तनूकृ॒थे मा नो॒ वि यौ॑ष्टं स॒ख्या मु॒मोच॑तम् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कथा । नूनम् । वाम् । विऽमनाः । उप । स्तवत् । युवम् । धियम् । ददथुः । वस्यःऽइष्टये ।  
> ता । वाम् । विश्वकः । हवते । तनूऽकृथे । मा । नः । वि । यौष्टम् । सख्या । मुमोचतम् ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 268

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विनौ विमनाः एतन्नामक ऋषिः नूनं पुरा कथा कथं वां युवाम् उप स्तवत् कथमुपास्तौत् । तेन स्तुतौ युवं युवां वस्यइष्टये । वस्यो वसीयः प्रशस्तं धनम् । तस्याभिलषितस्येष्टयेऽभिगमनाय यद्वा वसिष्ठधनस्येष्टये प्राप्तये धियं बुद्धिं विमनसे ददथुः अददाथां खलु । वस्य इति वसुमच्छब्दादीयसुनि ‘विन्मतोर्लुक् ' इति लुक् । ईयसुन ईकारलोपश्छांदसः ॥ तादृशौ युवां विश्वको हवते इति गतम् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, the seer of that name, Vimanas, formerly, how did he praise you? When he had praised you, you gave understanding to Vimanas for the attainment (इष्टये (*iṣṭaye*)) of वस्यः (*vasyaḥ*), that is, of the better, excellent wealth; that is, for the approach to that desired wealth; or else, for the obtaining of the best wealth. You indeed gave it. Such as you are, Viśvaka calls you; the rest has been explained already.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Aśvin deities) विमनाः (*vimanāḥ*)—the seer called Vimanas
- नूनं (*nūnaṃ*)—formerly
- कथा (*kathā*)—in what manner
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you
- उप स्तवत् (*upa stavat*)—praised
- युवं (*yuvaṃ*)—you (pleased with his praise, to him)
- वस्य इष्टये (*vasya iṣṭaye*)—for the way to the obtaining of wealth
- धियं (*dhiyaṃ*)—understanding
- दधथुः (*dadhathuḥ*)—gave
- ता (*tā*)—you who are thus well known
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you
- विश्वकः (*viśvakaḥ*)—the seer called Viśvaka
- तनूकृथे (*tanūkṛthe*)—for the sake of his son
- हवते (*havate*)—invokes with praises; therefore
- नः (*naḥ*)—in our case
- सख्या (*sakhyā*)—the friendship you have
- मा वि यौष्टं (*mā vi yauṣṭaṃ*)—do not cause it to depart, and
- मुमोचतं (*mumocataṃ*)—you who have come near us, unyoke the horses of your chariot

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, the seer called Vimanas formerly praised you in many ways. Pleased with his praise, you gave him understanding in the way of acquiring wealth. The seer called Viśvaka (the seer of this sūkta) invokes you, such as you are, with praises for the sake of his son. Do not let the friendship you have for us fall away. Unyoke here the horses of your chariot, you who have come near us.

##### English translation
How Vimanas once praised you, and you gave him understanding for the attainment of excellent wealth ! Vishwaka now invokes you for the

### Printed p. 269

sake of his son ; sever not our friendships, but fling loose ( your reins and gallop hither ).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यु॒वं हि ष्मा॑ पुरुभुजे॒ममे॑ध॒तुं वि॑ष्णा॒प्वे॑ द॒दथु॒र्वस्य॑इष्टये ।  
> ता वां॒ विश्व॑को हवते तनूकृ॒थे मा नो॒ वि यौ॑ष्टं स॒ख्या मु॒मोच॑तम् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> युवम् । हि । स्म । पुरुऽभुजा । इमम् । एधतुम् । विष्णाप्वे । ददथुः । वस्यःऽइष्टये ।  
> ता । वाम् । विश्वकः । हवते । तनूऽकृथे । मा । नः । वि । यौष्टम् । सख्या । मुमोचतम् ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पुरुभुजा पुरुभुजौ पुरूणां बहूनां धनादिदानेन भोजयितारौ । यद्वा । बहूनां स्तोतॄणां पालयितारावश्विनौ । युवम् । हि ष्म इत्यवधारणे । युवामेव इममेधतुम् । एधतेः ‘एधिवहोश्चतुः' ( उ. सू. १, ७८ ) इति चतुप्रत्ययः । चित्त्वादंतोदात्तः । इमां धनादिवृद्धिं विष्णाप्वे । विष्णोः सर्वेषां देवानां मुख्यत्वात् तद्ग्रहणे सर्वे देवा गृहीता भवंति । विष्ण्वादीन् कर्मणा व्याप्नोतीति विष्णापूः । पृषोदरादिः । यद्वा । विष्णुं सर्वग्रहेषु व्याप्तं सोमं दशापवित्रेणापुनातीति । तस्य चतुर्थी विष्णाप्व इति । एतन्नामके मम पुत्रे पौत्रे वा । पुत्राय पौत्राय वा । इमां धनादिवृद्धिं ददथुः अदत्तम् । किमर्थम् । वस्यइष्टये वसीयसः प्रशस्तधनस्येष्टय इच्छां पूरयितुम् ।' क्रियार्थोपपदस्य । (पा. सू. २. ३. १४ ) इति चतुर्थी । ता वाम् इति पूर्ववद्वयाख्येयम् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, पुरुभुजा (*purubhujā*): those who cause many to be fed through gifts of wealth and the like; or else, the protectors of many praisers. युवम् । हि ष्म (*yuvam | hi ṣma*) is in the sense of emphasis: you alone. This increase of wealth and the like you gave to विष्णाप्वे (*viṣṇāpve*).
